## 4. Sampling frame (measured, stratified)

| Element | Decision |
| ---- | ---- |
| Universe | AeroDataBox-covered airports per feed, measured from the pinned provider **FREE-TIER health/coverage endpoints** (`/health/services/feeds/{service}/airports` and/or `/health/services/airports/{icao}/feeds`). If the repository exposes `GET /api/v1/collection/coverage`, that is a **project-local aggregator**, not an AeroDataBox endpoint; it is acceptable only when repository evidence proves its provider inputs, pinned contract/version, cache policy, and hashes. Record `universeCount`, `catalogInUniverse`, `universeNotInCatalog` from the resulting frozen artifact. |
| Frame | Measured provider-covered universe with explicit feed eligibility. Keep zero-yield airports in the measured frame. `UNCLASSIFIED`, `UNMAPPED`, or feed-ineligible rows remain visible but cannot enter the corresponding primary stratum. Only a **confirmed** `coverage_failed` row leaves the feed-eligible frame. |
| Zero-yield / failure triage | `zero_yield_once` → keep + re-observe; `zero_yield_repeated` (≥2 valid complete empty observations) → keep + apply only the frozen soft penalty; `zero_yield_persistent` → keep + manual review. A provider/auth/rate-limit/timeout/parser error is never a zero. `coverage_failed` requires either authoritative provider coverage evidence or the pre-frozen repeated-failure confirmation rule on complete controlled checks; one transient error cannot remove an airport. Record cause, attempts, timestamps, response hashes, and adjudicator. |
| Primary strata | **traffic tier × macro-region** only (crossing more would explode cell count) |
| Balancing variables (reported within strata, NOT crossed) | network degree*, intl/domestic, carrier diversity*, time zone — from a FIXED reference snapshot at frame-build time, never from the recursive current sample |
| Catalog | Rebuild only from a versioned coverage artifact. `coverage_cache_ttl` is MEASURE→FREEZE from provider/account behavior; no hard-coded 12-hour refresh is binding. Every rebuild creates a new hash and never rewrites the prior frame artifact. |
| Tier mix | **`{HUB:1, MID:2, REGIONAL:1}`** per batch — **rotating anchor consumes the HUB slot** when anchor enabled (see §8.4) |
| Unit of prediction | **a flight-leg outcome** ("gate-out delay of leg L at cutoff C") |

### 4.1 Global traffic-tier assignment (binding)

Tier must be measured independently of Phase-6 outcomes; missing traffic data can never be replaced by blanket REGIONAL assignment.

| Item | Binding rule / value to freeze in `preprobe_reference_freeze_record` (NOT frozen until permitted reference data are obtained) |
| ---- | ---- |
| Traffic measure | MEASURE→FREEZE: exactly one metric must be selected before frame rebuild; record `traffic_metric_name`, units, and `traffic_metric_value` per airport; no OR choice survives FREEZE |
| Source | MEASURE→FREEZE: choose one actually accessible/licensed reference. OAG/Cirium access is NOT assumed. If no permitted source is available, `TRAFFIC_REFERENCE=BLOCKED` and final frame rebuild cannot pass |
| Reference period | **Single 12-month window ending ≤30 days before frame freeze** (e.g. `2025-08-04` through `2026-08-03` when frame freeze is `2026-09-02`) — never a rolling window that moves during Phase 6 |
| Thresholds | MEASURE→FREEZE: choose one deterministic percentile or absolute-cut algorithm with exact ties and missing policy; unresolved OR choices are forbidden at frame freeze |
| HUB definition | Satisfies HUB threshold AND `feed-eligible` |
| MID definition | Satisfies MID threshold AND not HUB |
| REGIONAL definition | Remainder **among tier-verified, reference-covered feed-eligible airports after HUB/MID assignment**. Airports with missing traffic reference remain `UNCLASSIFIED`, not REGIONAL. |
| Missing-reference policy | No traffic record → keep airport in the measured coverage universe but set `tier='UNCLASSIFIED'`, `tier_verified=false`, `tier_source='missing_reference'`; exclude from primary tier-stratified sampling until deterministically classified under the frozen reference/rule. Never silently relabel missing-reference airports REGIONAL. |
| Tie/boundary policy | Freeze exactly one algorithm. For absolute thresholds, equality enters the higher tier. For rank/percentile cuts, sort by `(traffic_metric DESC, ICAO ASC)` and freeze exact rank counts; ICAO resolves equal-metric rank order only. Never mix threshold and rank semantics. |
| Versioning | **After selection:** `traffic_source_name`, `traffic_source_version`, `traffic_retrieval_date`, `traffic_period`, `tier_version`, `tier_hash` (SHA-256 sorted ICAO→tier) in `adb_collection_meta` — *not frozen until chosen* |
| Required evidence | `reference_period`, `metric`, metric units, `hub_cut_metric`, `tier_cut_rule`, raw/reference hash, coverage percentage, missing-reference count, and sorted ICAO→tier hash |
| Rebuild | After freezing, **rebuild `clean.adb_sampling_frame`** (`tier`, `tier_source`, `traffic_prior`) and regenerate 18-cell counts before Gate 1/2. Do NOT derive tiers from Phase-6 delay outcomes |

Traffic reference values must be finite and nonnegative. A missing, nonfinite,
or negative value is invalid reference data: keep the airport visible in the
measured universe, set `tier='UNCLASSIFIED'`/`tier_verified=false`, and block
the affected reference/frame until the frozen missing-data rule resolves it.

`traffic_prior` multiplier is frozen as `HUB=3.0, MID=1.5, REGIONAL=1.0`; no alternative normalization may change anchor/adaptive ranking without a new documented freeze.

### 4.2 Macro-region mapping (binding)

Use the following six macro-regions; an ICAO-first-letter heuristic alone is insufficient:

1. **North America** (NA) — US, Canada, Mexico, the Caribbean, all ISO-defined Central American countries, and Greenland.
2. **Europe** (EU) — the explicit European country-code rows in the frozen mapping artifact, plus Turkey and Russian airports whose frozen reference longitude is `<60.000000°E`.
3. **Asia-Pacific** (AP) — the explicit East/South/Southeast/Central-Asian country-code rows in the artifact, **excluding Australia/NZ/Pacific islands** (those → OC) and the explicit Middle-East rows (those → MEA).
4. **Gulf/Africa** (MEA) — the explicit Middle-East and African country-code rows in the artifact. ICAO prefix examples (`O,H,F,D,G`) are QA hints only, not a complete classification rule.
5. **South America** (SA) — the explicit ISO-defined South-American country-code rows in the artifact; Central America is not included.
6. **Oceania** (OC) — Australia, New Zealand, and the explicit Pacific-island country-code rows in the artifact. A primary-frame airport maps to exactly one region; an unresolved airport stays `UNMAPPED` and is excluded rather than forced into a region.

| Item | Binding rule / value to freeze |
| ---- | ---- |
| Source | **ISO 3166 country → macro-region lookup (default, 1:1)**, primary key `ISO country code`; **with explicit airport/longitude overrides** (Russia split by 60°E, Turkey EU) in `region_overrides`. Missing/invalid country is `UNMAPPED`; an ICAO-prefix fallback is forbidden unless a separately versioned prefix table is explicitly frozen before Stage 1. |
| Mapping table | A versioned `country_code → macro_region` 1:1 artifact covering every country code observed in the frame (`region_mapping_version`, `region_mapping_hash`). A missing row, duplicate mapping, or unreviewed territory is `UNMAPPED`/BLOCKED; do not substitute an unstated country list. |
| Exceptions | Explicit override table: Turkey→EU, Greenland→NA, Australia→OC. Russia uses the frozen airport coordinate: longitude `<60.000000°E`→EU and `≥60.000000°E`→AP. Missing/invalid longitude→UNMAPPED. Never decide Russia from ICAO prefix alone. Both mapping and override tables are hashed. |
| Unknown policy | Missing/invalid country or unresolved override → `region='UNMAPPED'`, `region_source='missing'`, excluded from strata until manually classified; never silently assigned |
| Versioning | `region_mapping_source`, `region_mapping_version`, `region_mapping_retrieval_date`, `region_mapping_hash` in manifest |
| Enforcement | The actual production catalog builder must use the frozen artifact's country→region default plus explicit overrides/coordinates and no unstated ICAO heuristic. Recompute all 18 tier×region cells and assert at most one mapped region per ICAO; the Log must identify the real owner path/test. |

### 4.3 Population scope (binding)

Frozen for the entire pipeline with **parallel observability dimensions**: `population → snapshots → evaluation`, while webhook `capture` is measured independently and never gates snapshot existence (same scope unless another population is separately named):

| Category | Included in core experimental population? |
| ---- | ---- |
| Scheduled commercial passenger (operating flights with published schedule) | **YES** — core |
| Cargo (`withCargo`) | **NO** — excluded from core population; may be recorded as auxiliary `population_cargo` but not in primary denominator |
| Private / general aviation (`withPrivate`) | **NO** — excluded |
| Charter / non-scheduled | **EXCLUDE only when positively classified.** If the provider response does not expose a reliable scheduled/non-scheduled class for that record, set `scope_classification='unknown'`, retain it outside the confirmed-core count, and report/bound the ambiguity; never claim verified exclusion or silently include it as scheduled passenger. |
| Canceled (known canceled) | **YES, counted in population** — snapshot eligible, outcome=`canceled` |
| Diverted (actual destination ≠ scheduled) | **YES, counted** — snapshot eligible, outcome=`diverted`, `diversion_flag=true` |
| Codeshared marketing numbers | **NO as separate population rows** — deduplicated to operating leg (see §7.1 codeshare) |

FIDS query (see §5.1) uses `withCargo=false, withPrivate=false, withCodeshared=true` **to include all returned code-share statuses so internal canonicalization can attempt operating-leg resolution**; the provider contract is not assumed to supply complete operating↔marketing links. Then apply the frozen scope/canonicalization rules above.

### 4.4 PRE vs POST airport eligibility (binding)

**Decision (Option B — separate eligibility, explicit):**

- **PRE (provider-observable prediction population, S1):** requires `pre_eligible=true` (`feed_schedule` available) — an airport can only contribute `flight_population` / `flight_snapshots` at T-24h/T-6h/T-90m if schedule feed exists for that horizon.
- **POST/AIRBORNE:** requires `post_eligible=true` (`live` OR `adsb` feed) — an airport can only contribute `raw_airborne_events` / `flight_airborne_snapshots` if live/ADS-B feed exists.
- **Core experimental batch slots:** all four primary slots—1 HUB, 2 MID, 1 REGIONAL—require `in_frame && pre_eligible && post_eligible`. This makes each selected airport eligible to attempt both PRE and POST collection; it does **not** guarantee that either feed will yield observations. If a required tier/region has no dual-eligible candidate, the primary batch is `UNSAT/REFUSED`; never replace it with a structurally different POST-only airport.
- `PRE horizons never depend on an airport lacking required FIDS/schedule coverage` — controller refuses to schedule a T-24h snapshot for an airport where FIDS `withCargo/withPrivate` scope cannot be validated.

**POST-only auxiliary policy:** A `post_eligible=true, pre_eligible=false` airport may be studied only in a separately named, separately budgeted, explicitly human-authorized auxiliary run after core prerequisites pass. A POST-only auxiliary airport **cannot fill a core slot**, enter a crossover pair, influence primary adaptation/anchor ranking, or support denominator-based/deployment claims. Its rows carry `post_only_auxiliary=true` and remain outside every primary PRE/POST analysis. No auxiliary paid run is implied by this Plan.

Production controller requirement: the core selector **MUST** filter every tier with `WHERE in_frame=true AND pre_eligible=true AND post_eligible=true`, then enforce the frozen tier×region rule. Whether any repository is wired this way is an Implementation-Log evidence question, not a Plan claim.

### 4.5 Frame-balancing reference variables (binding)

| Variable | Source | Formula / definition | Reference period | Missing policy | Manifest fields |
| ---- | ---- | ---- | ---- | ---- | ---- |
| **Network degree** | Permitted/licensed scheduled-route reference (for example OAG, a lawfully retained historical FIDS-derived artifact, or airline schedule data), never the future Phase-6 population | Directed: `out-degree = distinct destinations with ≥1 scheduled departure/week`; `in-degree` similarly; `undirected degree = distinct connected airports either direction`; report all three, primary = undirected | Same 12-month window as traffic tier | No schedule data → `degree=NULL, degree_unverified=true` | `degree_source, degree_period, degree_threshold (≥1/week), degree_version` |
| **Carrier diversity** | Same external reference schedule (NOT `flight_population`) | `carrier_count = distinct operating carriers with ≥5% of departures at airport`; primary metric `effective carriers = 1/HHI` where `HHI=Σp_i²`; alternative `Shannon H=-Σp ln p` reported but not primary | Same 12-month | No data → NULL | `carrier_diversity_source, carrier_period, carrier_metric (1/HHI), carrier_threshold` |
| **International/domestic mix** | Same external reference (NOT `flight_population`) | `intl_share = international scheduled departures / total scheduled departures` (route-level intl = origin country ≠ destination country) | Same | NULL | `intl_source, intl_period` |
| **Exogenous traffic score** | Same traffic measure as §4.1 | `traffic_score = min(1, traffic_metric_value / hub_cut_metric)` normalized to [0,1], where `hub_cut_metric` is the numeric frozen HUB-boundary value (including the boundary value derived by a rank/percentile rule) | Same | NULL | as §4.1 |
| **Geo/network diversity score** | Derived from degree + intl_share + macro-region dispersion | `geo_score = 0.5*degree_norm + 0.3*intl_share + 0.2*region_rarity` (where `region_rarity = 1 - region_share`) — frozen formula | Same | NULL | `geo_formula_version` |
| **Carrier/international diversity score** | Carrier diversity + intl_share | `carrier_score = 0.6*effective_carriers_norm + 0.4*intl_share` — frozen | Same | NULL | `carrier_formula_version` |

**Traffic score saturation:** The `min(1, ...)` clipping means all airports at or above `hub_cut_metric` get `traffic_score = 1.0`, making the 40% traffic component intentionally coarse rather than a fine-grained ranking. Differentiation among same-tier airports comes from the other 60% (geo, carrier, yield). **FROZEN: linear clipped version** `traffic_score = min(1, traffic_metric_value / hub_cut_metric)`. The selected metric, units, boundary value, and tie/rank derivation are stored in the pre-probe freeze record; if `hub_cut_metric` is missing or non-positive, frame construction is BLOCKED. Log-scaling is not used because the coarse prior purpose does not require fine-grained differentiation and saturation prevents a single high-traffic airport from dominating the pool.

**Anchor normalization definitions:** The formulas above use `degree_norm` and `effective_carriers_norm` but do not define the normalization. **All normalization caps MUST be frozen BEFORE Gate 2 (before Stage 1 probes begin)** — Gate 0.5 may VERIFY them but must not choose them after observing probe outcomes. Freeze in the hash-locked `preprobe_reference_freeze_record` (created before any paid Stage-1 probe and later embedded unchanged into the final Phase-5 manifest):
- `degree_norm = min(1, degree / degree_cap)` where `degree_cap` = 90th percentile of the non-null degree distribution across frame airports (frozen from exogenous reference data BEFORE Stage 1).
- `effective_carriers_norm = min(1, effective_carriers / carriers_cap)` where `carriers_cap` = 90th percentile of the non-null effective-carriers distribution (frozen from exogenous reference data BEFORE Stage 1).
- `intl_share` is already [0,1] (proportion of international departures).
- `region_rarity = 1 - region_share` where `region_share` = proportion of mapped `in_frame` airports in the same macro-region (UNMAPPED rows are excluded from this denominator; zero mapped rows yields NULL).
- All normalization caps are frozen in the `preprobe_reference_freeze_record` before Stage 1 and re-verified (not re-chosen) at Gate 0.5. Record `anchor_norm_frozen_at` there and carry the same values/hash into the final manifest.

All balancing variables use the **fixed reference snapshot before frame freeze**, never the recursive current sample (§4).

For each balancing variable freeze `source`, `reference_period`, `formula`, `normalization`, coverage, missing policy, and source/hash before Stage 1. No value may be computed from Phase-6 sampled outcomes. Missing reference data yields `NULL` plus `<variable>_unverified=true`; it is never silently imputed into anchor scoring.

### 4.6 Recently observed diagnostic (binding)

`recently_observed_5d=true` means an airport's latest qualifying successful
direct observation is within five days of the current batch `window_start`.
Recompute it per batch from the actual immutable observation/event owner found
in the repository; never infer it from edge discovery or a failed/empty check.
Report count/share by tier×region. It is a diagnostic flag, not a frame,
eligibility condition, adaptation input, or claim of representativeness.

---

## 5. The provider-observable prediction population (S1)

**Why it exists:** the webhook is an event stream, not a complete population
enumeration. "These flights
emitted an update we captured" ≠ "these are all the flights that existed at the
cutoff." Observability selects on airport size, activity, disruption severity,
tracking quality, airline, region, ADS-B coverage — the very things we predict.

**Naming (Gate-0 fix):** the FIDS/schedule layer is called the
**"provider-observable prediction population"**, NOT a "true census". It is
"population relative to the validated AeroDataBox-supported operational frame."
**Gate 5** validates provider-population → reference operational source (e.g.,
FAA/BTS-derived schedules/ops for US flights, where available) → observed.

| Layer | Contents | Source |
| ---- | ---- | ---- |
| `flight_population` | append-only **population-observation membership** for a canonical flight instance returned by a particular role-aware FIDS query/retrieval. Logical uniqueness includes `population_query_id` (or source airport + service interval + retrieval identity), `flight_instance_id`, and `population_role`; repeated horizon/as-of retrievals never overwrite one another. For a prediction cutoff, use the latest qualifying observation whose durable `available_at≤prediction_cutoff_utc`; preserve `fids_retrieval_utc` separately and record staleness. | AeroDataBox FIDS/schedule for every planned airport+service interval; public tier/cost is only a candidate until re-verified and frozen for the actual account at Gate 0 |
| `flight_snapshots` | feature state at T−24h / T−6h / T−90m under §6.1 information-availability rules | built for every population+horizon-eligible flight, not only event-captured ones; missing features marked missing, never dropped |
| `flight_outcomes` | independent operational state plus per-target label status (§7) | terminal evidence from webhook and budgeted post-window retrieval |

**Corrected snapshot rule (the ONLY normative rule):** a snapshot exists iff the flight has a qualifying **provider-population observation available by the prediction cutoff** ∧ is eligible for the horizon. `population_member_at_cutoff` is an as-of derivation from append-only FIDS membership observations; a later/future FIDS response may never be used to backfill existence at an earlier cutoff. **Feature availability does NOT determine snapshot
existence.** Each feature's value enters the snapshot only if
`information_available_timestamp ≤ prediction_cutoff`; unavailable optional
features are `NULL` with `feature_missing=true`. A post-cutoff webhook
event is required for the LABEL only — it never decides whether the snapshot
exists.

**Coverage metrics (G5 + monthly):** for every horizon, by airport tier /
region / time-of-day / airline / tail-known:
report independent counters for population, horizon eligibility, expected/created snapshots, webhook capture, feature completeness/missingness, and target outcomes. Capture is not an upstream gate for snapshot creation.

**Denominator funnels:** The canonical rule is:
`snapshot_exists = population_member_at_cutoff AND horizon_eligible`, where `population_member_at_cutoff` means membership supported by the latest qualifying FIDS population observation with `available_at ≤ prediction_cutoff_utc`. Capture and feature availability are separate dimensions, NOT prerequisites for snapshot existence. A population+horizon eligible flight gets its snapshot row even when
webhook capture is zero or optional features are missing.

**PRE counters and exact rate denominators:**

| Counter/rate | Definition |
|---|---|
| `population_count` | qualifying as-of FIDS population members in the service interval |
| `horizon_eligible_count` | population members with constructible selected T and the named horizon |
| `snapshot_expected_count` | equals `horizon_eligible_count` |
| `snapshot_created_count / snapshot_expected_count` | builder completeness; missing webhook/features do not change expected rows |
| `webhook_captured_count / horizon_eligible_count` | parallel notification-capture rate; not downstream of snapshot creation |
| `required_features_complete_count / snapshot_created_count` | feature completeness under cutoff rules |
| `optional_feature_missing_count / snapshot_created_count` | count/rate with at least one named optional feature missing |
| `target_observed_count / target_applicable_snapshot_count` | target-specific label observability; cancellation may make movement targets not applicable |

Report every numerator and denominator, by horizon/airport/tier/region. A zero
denominator yields `rate=NULL` plus reason, never zero.

**AIRBORNE/POST uses one nested flight-level funnel, not a third population:**
`airborne_eligible → airborne_observed → airborne_usable →
airborne_trajectory_complete → POST_snapshot_eligible → POST_labeled`, exactly
as defined in §7.5. Report each transition as `next/current` and also against
`airborne_eligible`. Snapshot counts per flight are reported separately and do
not replace the flight-level denominator. The AIRBORNE/POST family is never
split into a capture-defined "AIRBORNE" population and a second "POST"
population.

**Coverage taxonomy uses independent dimensions, not one mutually exclusive state.** Record at minimum `in_frame`, `pre_eligible`, `post_eligible`, `subscription_started`, `delivery_received`, `provider_failure`, `zero_yield_state`, `captured_in_population`, `captured_outside_population`, `snapshot_created`, and `outcome_observed`, each with its applicable timestamp/count. Derived dashboard summaries may apply a documented display priority but never replace the source dimensions.

Plus per-frame **coverage-age distribution** (hours since last direct
observation, by tier/region) reported monthly and at G5. The mapping
`global frame vs directly observed vs edge-discovered` is kept explicit
end-to-end so the GNN never conflates "not observed" with "no edge" (§12.3).

**Population-claims rule:** until the provider-observable population layer is validated, all results
are "under the collection regime", never population-representative.

### 5.1 Complete FIDS protocol (binding)

**Current provider-contract pin (re-fetched, Sep1 correction):**

| Field | Pinned value |
| --- | --- |
| Public OpenAPI server/transport | RapidAPI gateway (`https://aerodatabox.p.rapidapi.com/`) for this public contract pin; this does **not** identify the user's subscribed channel |
| User subscription channel | `BLOCKED_LIVE` until Gate 0; verify whether the account is RapidAPI, API.Market, direct, or another authorized channel rather than inferring it from the public server URL or 60,000-unit entitlement |
| Retrieved at UTC | `2026-09-01T13:05:24Z` |
| API info version | `1.15.3.0` (OpenAPI document format `3.0.4`) |
| SHA-256 | `735620f2d2132c5bf51768f50caa767b7f0b25be8b128679641402666696890a` |
| Source URL | `https://doc.aerodatabox.com/docs/openapi-rapidapi-v1.json` |

Verified against this pin: `GET /flights/airports/{codeType}/{code}/{fromLocal}/{toLocal}` is the airport-FIDS endpoint. Live account limits and payload semantics still require Gate-0.5 evidence. Every parameter is frozen in `adb_collection_meta` (`fids_protocol_version`):

| Parameter | Frozen value |
| ---- | ---- |
| Endpoint | `GET /flights/airports/{codeType}/{code}/{fromLocal}/{toLocal}` (NOT `/flights/schedule`) — version recorded at Gate 0 (`fids_api_version`, `fids_docs_date`, `openapi_sha256`) |
| Direction | `direction=Arrival` or `direction=Departure` or `direction=Both` (single parameter, NOT `withDepartures`/`withArrivals`). Direction is task-specific: PRE departure-population calls use the requested-airport departure movement as the primary membership role; `Both` may be used for contextual/network acquisition but does not make every returned movement a primary denominator member. |
| `withLeg` | `true` — includes information from the **opposite movement** and returns `departure` + `arrival` rather than only `movement`. Every returned movement is stamped `population_role = requested_airport_primary / opposite_movement_context`; opposite-movement context cannot independently establish membership for this service-interval query unless it is separately observed through its own qualifying primary query. |
| `withCancelled` | `true` — canceled flights ARE population members (§4.3); preserves `Canceled`, `Diverted`, and `CanceledUncertain` as distinct states |
| `withCodeshared` | `true` — include all provider-returned code-share statuses for internal canonicalization. This does **not** guarantee complete operating↔marketing link semantics; `CodeshareStatus`/record evidence still controls resolution. |
| `CodeshareStatus` | `IsOperator` + `IsCodeshared` + `Unknown` — `Unknown` is genuinely ambiguous; perfect operating-leg dedup cannot be claimed; create `codeshare_resolution_status = resolved_operator / resolved_marketing / ambiguous_unknown` |
| Operating-leg ambiguity | `ambiguous_unknown` remains an explicit unresolved canonicalization class. Primary population reports `confirmed_operating_legs` and `ambiguous_codeshare_records` separately; no exact operating-leg denominator claim may silently coerce `Unknown` to operator/marketing. |
| Cargo/Private | `withCargo=false, withPrivate=false` — only if provider supports these filters; otherwise prove exclusion via alternative field |
| Charter/non-scheduled | Scope may not be distinguishable from FIDS alone unless `FlightType` field is proven available |
| `withLocation` | `false` for population calls (location not needed for membership; kept for webhook) |
| Local from/to | `fromLocal` / `toLocal` in **airport local time**, format `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm` (see §5.3); max permitted interval is plan-dependent — freeze `fids_max_range_for_actual_plan` from live account evidence |
| Edge inclusivity | The **internal canonical service interval is `[service_window_start_utc, service_window_end_utc)`**. Provider `fromLocal/toLocal` inclusivity is `MEASURE→FREEZE` at Gate 0.5 and is not asserted from OpenAPI. After retrieval, the client filters by the verified selected provider-native schedule timestamp into the internal half-open interval and deduplicates overlap from adjacent/split queries; Gate 0.5 must also demonstrate that this strategy does not omit boundary flights. |
| Pagination/truncation | No pagination; **if practical result limit observed** (e.g. 500-record cap), characterize through live evidence and implement generic recursive split strategy — do NOT assume `result truncated` flag exists in provider contract |
| 2×2h segment treatment | For noncontiguous windows: freeze `segment_1_start/end`, `gap`, `segment_2_start/end`, **the same frozen airport set and parent experiment day for both child segments**, subscription lifecycle, FIDS service intervals, population membership, and cost accounting — gap flights must NOT silently enter the exposed/captured denominator unless explicitly classified |
| REST attempts/timeout | Airport-FIDS is an idempotent GET: **maximum 3 total attempts** (initial + at most 2 retries), 15-second timeout per attempt. Retry only the pre-frozen transient classes (`429`, eligible `5xx`, connect timeout/reset), with provider `Retry-After` when supplied or bounded exponential backoff+jitter otherwise. Never retry auth/validation/schema/other `4xx`. Reserve/debit every physical attempt **before issuing the HTTP request**; exhausted category/rate budget returns DEFER/FAIL. Flight-Alert delivery is separate and always uses `maxDeliveryRetries=0`. |
| Webhook acknowledgement deadline | The pinned provider contract requires a successful `2xx` response to the webhook within **10 seconds**. The production handler must durably persist the raw envelope/items before returning `2xx`; timeout/non-2xx is a delivery failure and remains billable under the provider's SEND/retry rules. Re-verify the subscribed channel's current contract at Gate 0.5. |
| Schedule revisions | Capture `scheduledTime`, `revisedTime`, `predictedTime` per movement; preserve as-of versions (see §5.2); provider timestamps come from **provider clocks**, not airport/airline clocks |

**Required FIDS population provenance fields (P0):** every persisted query/result row records at minimum `source_airport_icao`, `query_direction`, `population_role`, `service_window_start_utc`, `service_window_end_utc`, original `fromLocal`, original `toLocal`, `airport_iana_timezone`, `scope_classification`, `codeshare_resolution_status`, `fids_retrieval_utc`, `response_hash`, and provider/API version. These fields are denominator provenance, not optional diagnostics.

**Timestamp model — provider clocks:** Preserve all provider clocks separately — do NOT collapse them:

| Timestamp field | Source | Definition | Applicability / NULL rule |
| ---- | ---- | ---- | ---- |
| `provider_notification_generated_utc` | Notification envelope `timestampUtc` | When the provider generated the notification | **Required on webhook delivery/item records; N/A/NULL on FIDS-only population records** |
| `delivery_attempt_seq_no` | `deliveryAttempt.seqNo` | Attempt sequence for this notification | **Required on webhook delivery attempts; N/A/NULL on FIDS-only records** |
| `delivery_attempt_utc` | `deliveryAttempt.timestampUtc` | When the provider attempted delivery | **Required on webhook delivery attempts; N/A/NULL on FIDS-only records** |
| `provider_state_updated_utc` | Flight item/contract `lastUpdatedUtc` when supplied | Time of latest provider flight-information update **excluding Location**; never use it as an upper/lower bound on `location_reported_utc` | Required only for record classes whose pinned contract supplies it; preserve NULL/N/A elsewhere rather than fabricating it |
| `location_reported_utc` | `location.reportedAtUtc` | When the location was reported | Required when a Location object exists under the pinned contract; otherwise NULL/N/A |
| `fids_retrieval_utc` | Our FIDS client | When the FIDS response was retrieved | **Required on FIDS query/result provenance; N/A/NULL on webhook-only records** |
| `http_received_at_utc` | Our HTTP layer | When we received a webhook delivery | Required on webhook ingress; N/A/NULL on FIDS-only rows |
| `raw_persisted_at_utc` | Durable raw insert | When immutable raw content committed | Required for raw ingress/query-response records while content is retained; record-class specific |
| `available_at` | Durable normalized/query-result availability | Earliest UTC instant the fact/result was durably usable for feature-building | Required for every normalized fact or population observation used in an as-of decision |
| `timestamp_source` | Provenance enum | Which real source clock supports a semantic event time | Required on semantic events; allowed values include `'provider_notification' / 'provider_state' / 'location_reported' / 'fids_retrieval' / 'received' / 'derived'` |

**Do NOT** automatically call `lastUpdatedUtc` = `provider_published_utc` without defining what "published" means. If retaining a generic `provider_published_utc` field, derive it explicitly from the appropriate source clock and preserve originals.

**Raw event timestamp NULLability:** Non-location state updates (e.g., status changes, schedule revisions) may not have `reportedAtUtc`. Therefore: (a) do NOT make a single location-style `event_timestamp` semantically mandatory for every event type, (b) define event-type-specific source timestamps and/or permit NULL where the provider supplies no real occurrence time, (c) never replace unknown occurrence time with receipt time without a source flag. Record `timestamp_source = 'provider_notification' / 'provider_state' / 'location_reported' / 'fids_retrieval' / 'received' / 'derived'` on each applicable semantic event/query-derived fact.

**Additional FIDS canonicalization/storage rules:**

| Rule | Binding behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Cancellations/diversions | Canceled → `status=canceled`; Diverted → `actual destination ≠ scheduled` with `diversion_flag`; CanceledUncertain → distinct state, NOT conflated with Canceled |
| Retimes | Revised schedule values never create a new physical-flight identity merely because time moved. Preserve append-only `schedule_version_id`/`retime_version` rows under the same stable `flight_instance_id`. Create a distinct flight instance only with positive evidence of a separate operated leg; unresolved cases remain `identity_resolution_status='ambiguous_distinct_leg'` and are bounded, not double-counted (§7.1). |
| Population membership | One **append-only membership observation** per qualifying `population_query_id × analytic_identity_id × population_role`, where `analytic_identity_id` is the confirmed `flight_instance_id` or the deterministic provisional identity for an unresolved code-share record, and the verified provider-native selected schedule value is in `[service_window_start_utc, service_window_end_utc)`. Repeated FIDS retrievals are distinct observations; never overwrite them. Horizon membership uses the latest qualifying observation with `available_at≤prediction_cutoff_utc`. Reports separate `confirmed_operating_leg_count` from `ambiguous_codeshare_record_count`; unresolved provisional identities cannot silently enter an exact physical-leg denominator. |
| Raw preservation | Persist raw JSON + `SHA-256` + `response_hash` + `retrieval_utc` + `fromLocal/toLocal` + `timezone`; while lawfully retained, raw records are **immutable** — no semantic UPDATE after initial persist. Compliance deletion/expiry under §10.2 is allowed and must leave a non-content audit tombstone/hash where permitted |
| Retrieval timestamp | `fids_retrieval_utc` is distinct from optional semantic `event_timestamp` and from every provider clock |

### 5.2 T−24h operational acquisition (binding)

It is **forbidden** to reconstruct T−24h from a final schedule observed later. T−24h is `selected_t_milestone − 24h`; that milestone remains BLOCKED until Gate 0.5 selects a constructible provider-native schedule field under §6.0. No FAA gate/runway alias fallback is permitted. Proven in two permitted ways:

**Preferred — live, versioned schedule-population snapshots:**
```text
future airport/window assignment FROZEN early enough to schedule the longest-horizon acquisition grid
  → for T-24h, materialize a pre-frozen query-time grid spanning the service-window cutoff interval
  → for T-6h, materialize the analogous pre-frozen query-time grid
  → for T-90m, materialize the analogous pre-frozen query-time grid
  → each query starts early by population_acquisition_guard and covers the frozen service interval (split only by verified provider range)
  → experimental webhook window (collection)
  → later outcomes
```
A request started exactly at a cutoff does **not** prove its response was usable by that cutoff. Before Phase 6, Gate 0.5 must MEASURE→FREEZE: (a) `population_acquisition_guard_minutes` from FIDS latency + scheduler/queue/rate-limit allowance + explicit safety margin, (b) `population_refresh_cadence_minutes`, and (c) `population_max_staleness_minutes`. For each horizon `H`, materialize a **non-adaptive retrieval grid** whose target cutoff span runs from `service_window_start_utc − H` through `service_window_end_utc − H`; each grid request starts at least the frozen guard before its target grid instant. Each retrieval covers the same frozen service interval (subject only to verified range splitting), so later-in-window flights can use a fresher pre-cutoff population observation rather than being judged only from the earliest flight's snapshot.

The actual membership rule remains decisive: for each flight/horizon select the **latest** qualifying population observation with durable `available_at ≤ prediction_cutoff_utc`; record `population_observed_at_utc` and `population_staleness_seconds`. If no qualifying observation exists, or if its staleness exceeds the frozen `population_max_staleness_minutes`, mark that flight/horizon `population_unobserved`/ineligible for the primary denominator rather than assuming absence. A slow response whose durable `available_at` crosses the flight cutoff is not eligible for that flight/horizon. A later FIDS query can never backfill an earlier cutoff and **can never backfill an earlier population** membership decision. The refresh cadence/guard/grid is frozen and budgeted before Phase 6 and may not adapt to outcomes, yield, or disruptions. `assignment_frozen_at` must be early enough to schedule the full guarded T−24h grid; otherwise that horizon is unavailable rather than reconstructed from future state.

**Alternative — historical versioned as-known-at-time (only if provider preserves genuine versioned history):**
If used, must prove provider retains trustworthy versioned `as-known-at-T` state + valid `available_at` timestamps, and ETL uses `information_available_timestamp ≤ T-24h` to fetch the version observable at T−24h. Proof required at Gate 0.5 before approval.

Intended paths: `scripts/build_stratified_catalog.ts` assignment, standalone `server/lib/disruption/fidsCensus_v3.ts` fetcher, and historical-store as-of lookup. Production wiring is **required but not yet evidenced**; this is an offline implementation blocker, not an exemption from implementation.

### 5.3 FIDS timezone / DST handling (binding)

Provider FIDS `fromLocal/toLocal` bounds are **airport-local time**. Fixed `UTC±offset` is forbidden.

```
canonical UTC experimental interval (e.g. window_start UTC, cutoff UTC)
  → airport IANA timezone (from airport metadata, e.g. America/Los_Angeles for KLAX)
  → convert to local date/time with DST-aware library (e.g. `Intl` / `date-fns-tz`)
  → correct fromLocal/toLocal strings (e.g. "2026-08-30T08:00")
  → provider query
  → persist original fromLocal/toLocal + IANA timezone + UTC equivalents (both)
  → persist conversion used
```

Mandatory DST test cases (in `tests/fidsTimezone.test.ts`): spring-forward gap, fall-back repeat hour, southern hemisphere, and `UTC window crossing midnight local`. Fail → controller refuses.

### 5.4 REST/FIDS materialized-calendar budget proof

Generate one machine-checkable call ledger from the frozen 31-batch-day calendar and account-verified maximum FIDS range; no hand estimate is accepted as budget proof:

```text
for each day, airport, **every frozen population-refresh grid time** for T-24h/T-6h/T-90m (each start shifted earlier by the frozen acquisition guard), and every 2×2 child segment:
  materialize requested half-open service interval + retrieval/as-of target time
  split by the verified account/provider max range
  FIDS_BASE_UNITS  += unsplit request units at the frozen current endpoint cost
  FIDS_SPLIT_UNITS += additional split-request units
then add separate capped retry, validation, outcome, history, and diagnostic ledgers

REST_TOTAL_UNIT_BUDGET = sum(all seven category caps)
```

**Central API-unit ledger:** Manifest records per-category hard caps:

```text
FIDS_BASE_UNITS               = generated, BLOCKED pending calendar
FIDS_SPLIT_UNITS              = generated, BLOCKED pending max range/calendar
FIDS_RETRY_UNIT_BUDGET        = frozen after generated base
VALIDATION_UNIT_BUDGET        = generated from frozen Gate-5 sample
OUTCOME_REST_UNIT_BUDGET      = BLOCKED_MEASURE_TO_FREEZE
HISTORY_BOOTSTRAP_UNIT_BUDGET = BLOCKED_MEASURE_TO_FREEZE
DIAGNOSTIC_UNIT_BUDGET        = BLOCKED_MEASURE_TO_FREEZE
REST_TOTAL_UNIT_BUDGET        = exact sum; must fit protected API allocation
```

Before issuing **any** billable REST request:

```text
if projected_total_units > protected_rest_budget:
    REFUSE / DEFER
```

All REST callers must pass through one central limiter that records the live-verified account rate limit, request category, attempt number, units reserved/settled, 429 response, backoff, and terminal decision. Every retry debits the category retry budget **before issuing its HTTP request**; exhausted retry/rate budget returns DEFER/FAIL rather than bypassing through another script. The actual account limit is `BLOCKED_LIVE_EVIDENCE`; central production integration is a mandatory offline requirement and is currently `NOT_VERIFIED_REPOSITORY_UNAVAILABLE` in the paired Log. Phase 6 cannot start with independent uncoordinated callers.

Never move API units between protected categories or treat Alert credits as REST units. A probe targets its frozen duration but ends early at its SEND-aware soft stop; record actual exposure and `duration_censored=true`. Cap censoring changes exposure reporting only and never redefines §9.2 yield. A probe truncated before its target duration cannot be called a complete 2-hour or 4-hour probe. If the generated REST total exceeds the protected API allocation, Phase 6 is NO-GO until the design is explicitly amended and re-frozen. A standalone file or helper is never production evidence; central limiter wiring and tests must be recorded in the Log.

---

## 6. Data pipeline & provenance (S2–S5)

| # | Delta | Rule |
| ---- | ---- | ---- |
| S2 | Normalized immutable ingress | `raw_delivery` stores the HTTP envelope/body/hash first; `raw_delivery_item` stores each raw flight item; `processing_attempt` stores parser/storage outcomes. `adb_ingest_events` is legacy mixed state and is not simultaneously the immutable envelope and processing record |
| S3 | Event log before current state | `flight_events` (one row per flight-item observation) feeds `flight_state` (latest via dedup) — the dedup table is an operational convenience, never the only research dataset |
| S4 | Provenance invariant | Never destructively overwrite lawfully retained research provenance. Rebuilding state from retained raw content must be possible **within its permitted retention horizon**; after compliance expiry, reproducibility relies on permitted derived records, hashes/tombstones, manifests, and transformations rather than unlawfully retained raw Contents. |

**Durable acknowledgement order (binding):** receive → authenticate/minimally validate → commit immutable `raw_delivery` (and raw items in the same transaction when required by the chosen transaction design) → send successful 2xx → semantic parse/state processing. Raw DB failure returns non-2xx; parser/semantic/state failure after durable raw persistence records a `processing_attempt` and leaves the raw payload recoverable. Migration-file existence alone does not prove this order is production-wired.

**Four separate identities (binding):**

| Identity | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| Raw delivery identity | Provider notification ID + delivery-attempt sequence where present; otherwise deterministic request/body hash plus attempt metadata. Retries remain distinct attempts linked to one notification |
| Raw item identity | `(raw_delivery_id, item_index, raw_item_sha256)`; preserves duplicate/retry provenance |
| Semantic observation identity | Versioned key over canonical flight instance, semantic event type/phase, available provider state/location clock(s), and raw-item hash. It must work for non-location events and multiple updates sharing a timestamp |
| Canonical flight-instance identity | Identity-v2 rule in §7.1; never interchangeable with notification, delivery, item, or semantic-event IDs |

`flight + carrier + locReportedUtc` is not a universal event identity. It may contribute to a location-observation key only when a real location clock exists.

### 6.0 Exact T milestone (binding; BLOCKED pending Gate-0.5 verification)

`T` in `T-24h / T-6h / T-90m` is **NOT generic "scheduled departure"**. It is represented only as `selected_t_milestone` until Gate 0.5 verifies whether any provider-native field constructs scheduled gate-out or scheduled wheels-off semantics. If neither is verified, `T_MILESTONE_SELECTED = BLOCKED` and Phase 6 remains NO-GO.

| Item | Frozen value |
| ---- | ---- |
| Exact milestone | `selected_t_milestone`: Gate 0.5 selects one constructible provider-native schedule field and records its semantics/path; otherwise BLOCKED. FAA gate/runway names are not candidates without equivalence evidence |
| Provider field path | Provider-native `departure.scheduledTime` is preserved without an FAA alias. Populate an alias only when Gate 0.5 proves semantic equivalence; otherwise alias `NULL`, `milestone_unverified=true` |
| Original vs revised | `T` uses the first observed value of the verified selected milestone; `revisedTime` never silently moves T |
| Service-date handling | `initial_service_date` is the origin-local date of the first verified provider-native schedule identity and is immutable key material. `current_scheduled_service_date` is versioned state. Neither depends on an unverified FAA alias. |
| Retime policy | The first verified provider-native schedule identity helps disambiguate same-number legs, but every later revision—including a ≥2-hour or local-date shift—is an append-only schedule version under the same physical `flight_instance_id` unless independent provider/operational evidence proves a distinct leg. Store original and current service date separately; ambiguous split cases are quarantined/bounded. |
| Timezone | Preserve provider-native local/UTC representations and derive UTC with the airport IANA timezone per §5.3; alias remains NULL if semantics are unverified |
| Stability | `selected_t_milestone` for a canonical `flight_instance_id` is frozen from its first qualifying verified provider-native schedule identity and never retroactively moved by later FIDS revisions. Later population observations are append-only as-of versions/evidence; they do not overwrite the frozen T or prior membership observations. |

**Midnight rotations:** When a flight crosses midnight (e.g., departs 23:00 UTC, arrives 01:00 UTC the next day), the following rules apply:
1. **Service date:** use the origin-local date of the verified provider-native schedule identity. Do not derive it from an unverified gate/runway alias.
2. **Flight instance ID:** Uses `initial_service_date` from the origin, not the destination. A flight initially scheduled from IAD on Aug 31 and arriving LHR on Sep 1 has `initial_service_date = 2026-08-31`; later retimes update versioned current schedule state, not the physical-flight key.
3. **Crossover windows:** Assign flights using the verified provider-native selected schedule value, never an unverified gate/runway alias.
4. **Cutoff handling:** Compute T-24h/T-6h/T-90m only from frozen `selected_t_milestone`; before that freeze, cutoff construction is BLOCKED.
5. Record `midnight_crossing = true/false` on each flight for audit.

`T-24h` means `T minus 24h`, `T-6h` minus 6h, and `T-90m` minus 90 minutes, all computed from frozen `selected_t_milestone` UTC. There is no forced fallback: without a constructible selected field, cutoff creation is BLOCKED.

### 6.1 Dual prediction-state data contract (PRE + AIRBORNE)

The plan has **two distinct prediction states**, never merged into one row.
**An event is NOT one of them.** `prediction_state` is a property of the
**snapshot / training example**, derived at dataset-construction time; the raw
 event carries only immutable provider/source facts (`event_phase`, canonical
source clocks, `timestamp_source`, `data_stage PRE|POST`); optional semantic
`event_timestamp` may be NULL. The same observation may feed
features for multiple tasks → we never burn an event's reusability by stamping
it with one state. The two derived states:

```text
prediction_state = PRE_DEPARTURE        cutoffs: T-24h / T-6h / T-90m
                                        valid under frozen provider-native PRE criterion
                                        target: gate-out delay / arrival disruption /
                                                cancellation / diversion

prediction_state = AIRBORNE             state_observation_time_utc = provider location/state time
                                        prediction_cutoff_utc = deployable decision/as-of time,
                                        normally this item's `available_at` after durable ingestion
                                        valid only under independently verified provider-native airborne
                                        movement evidence; otherwise denominator BLOCKED
                                        target: landing ETA, remaining time from prediction_cutoff_utc,
                                                selected-primary delay, P(selected_primary_delay>15/60), diversion risk
```

**The cutoff rule is universal:** for BOTH states, a feature may enter only when its **information availability** is no later than the deployable decision cutoff. PRE uses the frozen T−24h/T−6h/T−90m decision cutoff. AIRBORNE stores two times that must never be conflated: `state_observation_time_utc` (when the aircraft state/location applies) and `prediction_cutoff_utc` (when our system can actually make the prediction, normally that observation item's `available_at`). The current observation is therefore eligible for its own AIRBORNE snapshot without pretending the system knew it before receipt. Source latency is `prediction_cutoff_utc − state_observation_time_utc`. Actual departure may be used only if its information was available by the AIRBORNE decision cutoff; actual arrival remains a future label.

**Availability-time rule (NEW, blocking — the deepest leakage fix):** "the
event happened before the cutoff" is NOT sufficient. A model predicts from
what the **system could actually know at the decision time**, so the eligible
condition is:

```text
feature eligible  iff  information_available_timestamp ≤ prediction_cutoff
```

where `information_available_timestamp` = when OUR system had the value (the
time a downstream feature could actually be built from it). This is stricter
than `event_timestamp ≤ prediction_cutoff`, which only proves the thing
happened, not that we knew it. Every preserved observation carries provider-native
timestamps and they are NEVER conflated:

```text
provider_notification_generated_utc  when the provider generated the notification
provider_state_updated_utc          when the provider's internal state was last updated
location_reported_utc               when the location was reported (nullable for non-location events)
http_received_at_utc                when our HTTP layer received the raw delivery
raw_persisted_at_utc                when raw envelope was persisted (BEFORE 2xx)
available_at                        when our ingestion made it usable for feature-building
```

Example that must NOT leak: `location_reported_utc = 14:00`, `provider_state_updated_utc =
14:01`, `http_received_at_utc = 14:06`, `raw_persisted_at_utc = 14:06:01`, `available_at = 14:07`. With a
prediction cutoff of 14:05 the observation is INELIGIBLE even though
`reportedAtUtc ≤ 14:05`, because `available_at = 14:07 > 14:05`. This
discipline applies uniformly to: flight events, FIDS/schedule revisions, live
flight status, weather observations & forecasts (TAF amendments),
network/congestion features, and tail/rotation features (previous-leg arrival
must be both landed AND its arrival value available, by cutoff). For a source fact, `available_at` is the earliest time the fact was durably usable by the system. For a deterministic derived feature, preserve three clocks: `input_knowledge_available_at=max(required_input.available_at)`, `online_feature_available_at` when an already-deployed transform actually made the feature usable (nullable for offline replay), and `materialized_at` for the later table build. Eligibility requires every input to pass the cutoff and the exact transform version to have been frozen/deployable by the cutoff; an online feature additionally requires `online_feature_available_at≤cutoff`. Offline replay may use the frozen transform over cutoff-eligible inputs, but later materialization never changes what was knowable. No availability clock is backfilled from a final dataset.

### 6.2 Airborne data foundation (binding preservation contract)

The pinned provider schema contains candidate notification/location fields,
but this document audit had no repository or live payload with which to prove
the production parser, account coverage, or field frequency. Treat the list
below as a **required preservation contract**, not a claim that current code
already implements it. Gate 0.5 must preserve and verify the provider-native
paths/semantics actually observed; absent fields stay NULL with provenance.

**Hard requirement:** do not reduce the webhook payload to "latest location"
before durable raw storage. `flight_events` keeps one semantic observation per
raw item/version so a trajectory can be reconstructed within lawful retention.
No mutable upsert key—including any key based only on flight, carrier, or
`lastUpdatedUtc`—may be the raw or trajectory identity. S5 uses the layered
identity in §6; location rows include the real location clock plus raw-item
provenance, and non-location observations use applicable state/type clocks and
the raw-item hash. A `flight_state` table may hold latest state only as a
derived convenience and is never trajectory evidence.

**Per-observation fields to preserve (raw collection layer):**

| Group | Fields |
| ---- | ---- |
| Identity | flight_number, callsign, carrier, aircraft_reg, aircraft_mode_s, aircraft_model, icao24 where available |
| Times | canonical §6.4 clocks: notification generation, delivery attempt sequence/time, provider state update, nullable location report, HTTP receipt, durable raw persistence, `available_at`, and `timestamp_source`; optional semantic `event_timestamp` never substitutes for missing provider time |
| Scheduled/revised | dep_scheduled_utc, dep_revised_utc, arr_scheduled_utc, arr_revised_utc, original/current/actual destination |
| Movement chain | Eight nullable FAA/ASPM-concept aliases: `scheduled_gate_out`, `actual_gate_out`, `scheduled_wheels_off`, `actual_wheels_off`, `scheduled_wheels_on`, `actual_wheels_on`, `scheduled_gate_in`, `actual_gate_in`. Preserve provider-native movement fields separately. Populate an alias only after §6.3 verifies exact gate/runway semantics and, for `actual_*`, actuality; otherwise NULL + reason. Never populate two distinct concepts from one ambiguous provider field or derive gate from runway. |
| Airborne state | lat, lon, altitude_ft, pressure_altitude_ft, pressure_hpa, ground_speed_kt, true_track_deg, vsi_fpm, on_ground, flight_phase |
| Derived downstream (**NOT raw provider fields**) | `distance_to_destination`, `distance_flown`, `fraction_of_route_completed` only in clean/trajectory/snapshot layers, with transform version and the three derived-feature clocks defined in §6.1. |
| Context | weather_snapshot_id, eta_provider, eta_model_reference |
| QC | data_quality_flag, trajectory_gap_seconds, source_latency_seconds |

**Milestone-mapping verification (NEW, required before schema freeze — do NOT
blindly rename provider fields):** AeroDataBox exposes
`FlightAirportMovementContract` with `scheduledTime`, `revisedTime`,
`predictedTime`, `runwayTime`, `gate`, and other movement fields. That DOES NOT
automatically mean every provider `scheduledTime` equals FAA's
`scheduled_gate_out` or `scheduled_wheels_off`. Before the milestone schema is
frozen (and re-confirmed in the Gate 0.5 sample inspection), verify the ACTUAL
provider JSON semantics for each of the eight §6.2 milestones:
`mapped_<milestone> = <provider field path> + <semantics note>` (which
milestone it counts as and any caveat), recorded in code/config, so a provider
`scheduledTime` is never renamed `scheduled_gate_out` unless exact gate
semantics are supported. A single ambiguous `scheduledTime` may populate **at
most one** of the gate/runway aliases after verification; it cannot be copied
into both. Verify runway semantics from the pinned contract and live payload,
then separately verify whether each value is actual or estimated. Gate-out and
gate-in aliases require an independent verified gate source. This milestone
mapping is a binding §6.2 requirement; Gate 0.5 re-verifies it on live payloads
(§17 step 16). **Actuality is a separate P0 check:** current provider `revisedTime` and `runwayTime` can represent actual **or estimated** times. An `actual_*` FAA/ASPM alias is populated only when both (a) gate/runway semantic equivalence and (b) actual-vs-estimated status are verified for that observation. Otherwise keep the alias NULL with `milestone_unverified=true` and/or `actuality_unverified=true`; never turn an estimate into an actual label. If a milestone cannot be verified, it remains NULL rather than approximated.

**Flight phase (explicit):** `pre_departure, taxi_out, airborne_climb,
airborne_cruise, airborne_descent, approach, landed, taxi_in, gate_in` — so ETA
at 10 min after takeoff is never pooled with ETA 100 NM from destination.

**Trajectory QC / provenance (S5):** never overwrite raw points; pipeline is
`raw_airborne_events → clean_airborne_points → flight_trajectory →
flight_airborne_snapshots → Model POST`. Cleaning removes impossible
lat/lon/alt/speed, sorts timestamps, joins flight identifiers, marks
unjoinable points — mirroring the Springer 2024 in-flight-ETA study (§19).

**Observation cadence policy:** do NOT assume a fixed cadence (the provider
sends on update, not on a guarantee). Define and measure:
`post_departure_observation_policy` with target cadence, minimum acceptable,
and max gap; report `obs_per_flight, median_gap_seconds, P95_gap_seconds,
max_gap_seconds, trajectory_duration, trajectory_completeness_%`. Measure in
the canary (Gate 0.5) and monthly. **Only if measured cadence is insufficient
do we consider supplementary REST — never before measurement.**

**Operational state vs target-label terminalization (P0):** the flight-level operational lifecycle may reach a provider terminal state, cancellation, diversion, landing, or gate-in; **target labels do not all terminalize at the same first event**. Each target (`gate_out`, `wheels_off`, `wheels_on`, `gate_in`, cancellation/diversion, POST ETA/final-delay target) terminalizes independently under §7.3–§7.4 using its own verified actual milestone evidence and recovery deadline. For example, observing `actual_wheels_on` may close the wheels-on target while `gate_in` remains pending. Never finalize gate-in at landing merely because the flight is operationally terminal; censor/missing only after that target's documented recovery deadline.

**Flight-level disruption flags are not disruption events.** Cancellation evidence, diversion evidence, target-specific severe delay, weather severity, and verified ATC-program exposure remain separate per-flight/context fields. `CanceledUncertain` is not promoted to confirmed cancellation, and unverified FAA milestone aliases are not used to manufacture severe-delay flags.

**Multi-flight disruption engine: DEFERRED for Month 1.** No `disruption_event_id`, Engine-E event partition, or event oversampling may be produced until a later freeze records the named event/ATC/storm sources, source versions, event start/end, membership, merge/split rules, deterministic event-ID hash inputs, and tests keeping the whole event in one partition. Until then, `disruption_event_id=NULL`, event sampling is OFF, and no collection of individually disrupted flights is described as a multi-flight event. Deadline: before any Model-6/Engine-E fit or event-regime collection.

**Censoring timeout/grace (NEW, tightening):** "our collection window ended"
≠ "the flight never produced an outcome." A flight is only labeled
target-specific `label_status=censored` or `label_status=missing` only AFTER a separately-defined grace
interval past the last observation (or past the window end) during which a
terminal event could still arrive:

```text
last observation / window end
        │
        ▼
wait grace interval (defined once at freeze; proposal: 60 min, or
provider-typical arrival-notification latency + margin, measured at Gate 0.5)
        │
        ▼
terminal evidence arrived? → set the affected target `label_status=observed`
before deadline, absent?   → `label_status=pending`
deadline elapsed, absent?  → `label_status=missing` (or `censored` only for a valid survival endpoint)
```

Until that grace interval elapses the label is NOT final; the ETL never
finalizes a label early. Target-specific `label_status=missing` requires its
deadline to elapse with no usable terminal evidence. The grace value is frozen with the manifest (§17 Phase 5), and the
canary measures actual arrival-notification latency to justify it.

**Airborne snapshots table (S5):**

```text
  flight_airborne_snapshots:
  flight_instance_id | airborne_snapshot_id | prediction_state='AIRBORNE'
  provider_notification_generated_utc | delivery_attempt_seq_no | delivery_attempt_utc
  provider_state_updated_utc | location_reported_utc? | http_received_at_utc
  raw_persisted_at_utc | available_at | timestamp_source
  icao24 | registration | callsign | flight_number | airline | aircraft_type
  origin | destination | scheduled_departure | scheduled_arrival
  scheduled_gate_out | actual_gate_out | scheduled_wheels_off | actual_wheels_off
  scheduled_wheels_on | actual_wheels_on | scheduled_gate_in | actual_gate_in
  latitude | longitude | altitude | ground_speed | heading | vertical_rate | on_ground | flight_phase
  weather_snapshot_id | weather_timestamp_utc
  distance_to_destination | distance_flown | fraction_of_route_completed
  eta_provider | eta_model_reference | current_operational_destination
  data_quality_flag | trajectory_gap_seconds | source_latency_seconds
```

**POST labels — targets are milestone-explicit (NEW, blocking):** "scheduled
departure/arrival" is ambiguous, so every label names its exact milestones
(FAA-ASPM definitions, §6.2). Two delay families and one ETA family, all
specified as candidates, but constructible only when both scheduled and observed semantics are verified:
- **POST-A — landing timestamp / remaining flight time:** absolute landing target = verified `actual_wheels_on`; primary remaining-time label = `actual_wheels_on − prediction_cutoff_utc`. A separate diagnostic may measure remaining physical time from `state_observation_time_utc`, but it is not the deployable decision-time target.
- **POST-B1 — landing delay (wheels-on):** `label_arr_delay_wheels_on =
  actual_wheels_on − scheduled_wheels_on`
- **POST-B2 — gate-arrival delay (traveler-facing):** `label_arr_delay_gate_in =
  actual_gate_in − scheduled_gate_in`
Evaluated separately (SJSU airborne-ETA work supports the ETA target; §19);
`label_arr_delay` alone is deprecated because it does not say which milestone it
means. PRE-side targets are equally explicit: departure delay = `actual_gate_out
− scheduled_gate_out` (gate, traveler-facing) AND/OR `actual_wheels_off −
scheduled_wheels_off` (airborne/trajectory), stored as separate columns, never
merged into one ambiguous "dep delay".

### 6.3 Eight OOOI/ASPM milestone mapping (binding)

Each of the eight aliases maps one FAA/ASPM concept to one verified provider-native path, or remains NULL. The table lists **candidates to test**, not pre-approved mappings. Gate 0.5 verifies them before freeze. Never invent gate time from runway time, copy one ambiguous value into two concepts, or treat an estimate as actual.

| # | Milestone | Provider JSON path (provider-native) | Provider description | Semantic interpretation | FAA/ASPM equivalence | Null if unverified | Availability timestamp |
| ---- | ---- | ---- | ---- | ---- | ---- | ---- | ---- |
| 1 | `scheduled_gate_out` | `departure.scheduledTime` (scheduled departure — gate or runway ambiguous in provider) | Scheduled departure | Gate-out scheduled IF gate source verified; else runway scheduled | `scheduled_gate_out` per ASPM **only if gate semantics verified** | `milestone_unverified=true` until Gate 0.5 verifies gate vs runway | `available_at` of that movement |
| 2 | `actual_gate_out` | `departure.revisedTime` OR provider-native field shown by Gate 0.5 to represent gate departure | Provider may expose revised/estimated/actual time | Gate-out actual only when **gate semantics AND actuality (not estimate)** are verified | `actual_gate_out` **only if both are verified** | NULL + `milestone_unverified=true`/`actuality_unverified=true` otherwise | `available_at` |
| 3 | `scheduled_wheels_off` | `departure.scheduledTime` (when runway semantics verified) | Scheduled departure | Runway scheduled | `scheduled_wheels_off` | NULL if unverified | `available_at` |
| 4 | `actual_wheels_off` | `departure.runwayTime` | Provider runway departure time may be actual or estimated | Wheels-off actual only when runway semantics **and actuality** are verified | `actual_wheels_off` only if verified actual | NULL + `actuality_unverified=true` when estimate/unknown | `available_at` |
| 5 | `scheduled_wheels_on` | `arrival.scheduledTime` (when runway semantics verified) | Scheduled arrival | Runway scheduled | `scheduled_wheels_on` | NULL | `available_at` |
| 6 | `actual_wheels_on` | `arrival.runwayTime` | Provider runway arrival time may be actual or estimated | Wheels-on actual only when runway semantics **and actuality** are verified | `actual_wheels_on` only if verified actual | NULL + `actuality_unverified=true` when estimate/unknown | `available_at` |
| 7 | `scheduled_gate_in` | `arrival.scheduledTime` (scheduled arrival — gate or runway ambiguous) | Scheduled arrival | Gate-in scheduled IF gate source verified | `scheduled_gate_in` **only if gate semantics verified** | NULL | `available_at` |
| 8 | `actual_gate_in` | `arrival.revisedTime` (only when Gate 0.5 verifies gate semantics) | Provider revised arrival may be estimated or actual | Gate-in actual only when **gate semantics AND actuality** are verified | `actual_gate_in` **only if both are verified** | NULL + `milestone_unverified=true`/`actuality_unverified=true` otherwise | `available_at` |

For each: record a short provider-description paraphrase, caveat, pinned-contract hash/path, live payload evidence hash, parser path/commit, and reviewer. If unverifiable, keep NULL + `milestone_unverified=true`; never approximate. Gate 0.5 must test all eight candidates but may legitimately freeze some or all as unavailable. If selected T or the primary target remains unconstructible, the affected experiment is BLOCKED.

### 6.4 Timestamp taxonomy (binding)

Every observation preserves provider-native timestamps separately, NEVER conflated.
Do NOT equate `lastUpdatedUtc` with `provider_published_utc` without defining what
"published" means. Non-location state changes may not have `reportedAtUtc`.

| Timestamp / field | Source | Applicability / nullable rule | Ordering semantics | QC rule |
| ---- | ---- | ---- | ---- | ---- |
| `notification_id` | Webhook envelope `id` | **Required for webhook delivery/item records; NULL/N/A on FIDS-only records** | Stable across retry attempts; not unique per attempt | NOT a flight ID. Raw webhook-attempt identity additionally includes `delivery_attempt_seq_no`. |
| `provider_notification_generated_utc` | Notification envelope `timestampUtc` | **Required for webhook delivery/item records; NULL/N/A on FIDS-only records** | Provider clock; no strict cross-system order assumed without clock-skew allowance | Preserve exactly. |
| `delivery_attempt_seq_no` | Provider `deliveryAttempt.seqNo` | **Required for webhook delivery attempts; NULL/N/A on FIDS-only records** | Monotonic per notification | Together with `notification_id` identifies the webhook attempt. |
| `delivery_attempt_utc` | Provider `deliveryAttempt.timestampUtc` | **Required for webhook delivery attempts; NULL/N/A on FIDS-only records** | Provider clock | Preserve exactly; compare to our receipt only as latency QC with frozen clock-skew tolerance. |
| `delivery_attempt_cost_credits` | Provider `deliveryAttempt.costCredits` | **Required on received webhook attempts under the pinned contract; NULL/N/A on FIDS-only records** | Nonnegative | Missing on an applicable webhook attempt under the pinned contract is a contract/schema mismatch; never require this field on FIDS rows. |
| `provider_state_updated_utc` | Item/flight contract `lastUpdatedUtc` when supplied | **Required only for record classes whose pinned contract supplies it; otherwise NULL/N/A** | Provider flight-info clock **excluding Location** | Never call it publication time; do not require it to be ≥ `location_reported_utc`. |
| `location_reported_utc` | `location.reportedAtUtc` | **Required when a Location object exists under the pinned contract; otherwise NULL/N/A** | Independent location clock | It may be later than `provider_state_updated_utc` without being invalid. |
| `fids_retrieval_utc` | Our FIDS client | **Required on FIDS query/result provenance; NULL/N/A on webhook-only records** | Our client clock | Distinct from provider semantic clocks and from `available_at`. |
| `http_received_at_utc` | Our HTTP layer receipt | **Required on webhook ingress; NULL/N/A on FIDS-only records** | Our clock | On webhook ingress, must precede/equal our own durable raw-persistence clock. |
| `raw_persisted_at_utc` | Our durable raw insert | Required for retained raw ingress/query-response content; record-class specific | Our clock | Enforce same-system persistence ordering only for the applicable ingestion path. |
| `available_at` | Durable normalized/query-result availability | **Required for every normalized fact or population observation used in an as-of decision** | Our deployable information-availability clock | Leakage gate: `available_at ≤ prediction_cutoff_utc`; derived feature availability is max input availability, not offline materialization time. |
| `materialized_at` | Offline/online feature-table build | Optional audit clock | Processing/audit time only | Never substitute for `available_at`. |
| `timestamp_source` | Derived provenance | Required for semantic/query-derived facts where a source clock is represented | — | `'provider_notification'` / `'provider_state'` / `'location_reported'` / `'fids_retrieval'` / `'received'` / `'derived'`. |

Preserve any notification-envelope balance field as provider-reported diagnostic metadata only; it may represent an expected/pre-send value. Final authoritative reconciliation is settled `GET /subscriptions/balance`, not the envelope balance.

**Rules:**
- `event_timestamp` is optional semantic occurrence time. Non-location state updates may legitimately have no location-style occurrence time.
- Never replace an unknown provider occurrence time with receipt/persistence time without an explicit source/derivation flag.
- Do **not** impose `location_reported_utc ≤ provider_state_updated_utc`; provider documentation explicitly excludes Location from `lastUpdatedUtc` semantics.
- Cross-system provider-vs-our-clock comparisons are latency/QC checks with a frozen `clock_skew_tolerance_seconds`, not semantic ordering truths. Impossible/large negative latency beyond that tolerance is WARN/QUARANTINE per the frozen rule.
- Same-system ordering is **record-class specific**: webhook ingress requires `http_received_at_utc ≤ raw_persisted_at_utc ≤ available_at`; FIDS/query-result ingress uses its own retrieval/persistence path (for example `fids_retrieval_utc ≤ raw_persisted_at_utc ≤ available_at`) and must not fabricate webhook clocks.
- If a real semantic `event_timestamp` exists, `available_at < event_timestamp` is a QC anomaly; it does not justify fabricating a different event time.

### 6.5 Snapshot provenance (binding)

Every `flight_snapshots` and `flight_airborne_snapshots` row carries deterministic provenance:

`snapshot_provenance = { flight_population_row_id/hash, fids_response_hash, schedule_version, weather_snapshot_id, historical_feature_row_ids (array), feature_builder_version (git SHA), snapshot_builder_version }`

A researcher can answer "why was this feature available at this cutoff?" by joining `historical_feature_store` rows with `available_at ≤ cutoff`. Provenance stored as `provenance_json` or `provenance_hash` column.

### 6.6 Gate-0.5 measure→freeze items (binding)

Do NOT guess these — measure in canary/Gate 0.5, then freeze in manifest before Phase 6:

| Item | How measured at Gate 0.5 | Frozen in manifest |
| ---- | ---- | ---- |
| Censoring grace | Distribution of terminal-notification latency using a **verified actual terminal milestone** (prefer verified-actual wheels-on; otherwise another predeclared verified terminal clock). If no actual terminal clock is constructible, grace measurement is `BLOCKED`, not estimated from an estimated timestamp. | `censoring_grace_minutes` (MEASURE→FREEZE; proposal ≥60 only as a non-binding starting value) |
| `airborne_usable` min points | `obs_per_flight` distribution | `airborne_usable_min_points` (proposal ≥2 for line, ≥5 for curve) |
| Target cadence | `median_gap_seconds` | `target_cadence_seconds` |
| Minimum acceptable cadence | P95 gap | `min_acceptable_cadence_seconds` |
| Maximum gap | max | `max_gap_seconds` |
| Minimum trajectory duration/coverage | `trajectory_duration` + `completeness_%` | `min_trajectory_duration_seconds`, `completeness_threshold_%`, `completeness_formula` |
| Warning/fail rule | Thresholds breach → `WARN` vs `FAIL` | `cadence_warning_rule`, `cadence_fail_rule` |
| Population acquisition guard | Measure FIDS request/response latency plus scheduler/queue/rate-limit allowance; freeze an explicit safety margin and replay a response that crosses cutoff | `population_acquisition_guard_minutes` |
| Population refresh cadence | On a predeclared sample, characterize schedule appearance/revision timing and combine with REST budget/provider limits; choose a cadence before Phase 6 without using outcomes/yield | `population_refresh_cadence_minutes` |
| Population staleness ceiling | Define the maximum age of the latest qualifying FIDS population observation allowed for primary horizon membership; test a flight whose only available observation is older than the ceiling | `population_max_staleness_minutes` |

Gate 0.5 records sample size, all distributions, latency, and rationale paragraph before freeze.
