## 7. Flight operational state, target label status & modeling populations

The former five-state `outcome_state` mixed operational conditions with label availability and is retired. Store two independent dimensions:

| Dimension | Allowed values | Rule |
| ---- | ---- | ---- |
| `flight_operational_state` | `scheduled / departed / arrived / canceled / canceled_uncertain / diverted / unknown` | Provider-observable operational state. Diversion and arrival evidence may coexist; retain the strongest evidence and explicit flags rather than erasing an observed landing |
| `label_status` (one per target) | `pending / observed / censored / missing / not_applicable` | Target-specific availability. Before grace expiry an absent label is `pending`, not censored. After the target's deadline, classify from terminal evidence and censoring rule |

- Per-flight retained fields: `original_scheduled_destination`,
  `current_operational_destination`, `actual_destination`, `diversion_flag`, plus `flight_instance_id` (see §7.1).
- Per-task label populations are **target-specific** (see §7.3): e.g. `wheels_on` delay regression = only snapshots where `wheels_on_label_observed=true`.

### 7.1 Canonical `flight_instance_id` (binding)

One **confirmed physical operated flight leg** = one primary analytic prediction unit, not one marketing number or one schedule revision. Because provider code-share and distinct-leg status can be ambiguous, every raw/FIDS record first receives a deterministic `provider_flight_record_id`; canonical identity then carries `identity_resolution_status = confirmed_operating_leg / ambiguous_codeshare / ambiguous_distinct_leg`. A provisional identity supports linkage but is not counted as a confirmed physical leg until resolved or bounded under the frozen ambiguity rule.

| Component | Source | Role |
| ---- | ---- | ---- |
| Provider `flight.id` / `flightId` | **NOT ASSUMED TO EXIST OR BE STABLE** — notification envelope `id` is notification ID, not flight ID | Excluded from canonical identity. A future independently verified stable identifier may be stored as an optional attribute but is not a Phase-6 dependency |
| Canonical key v2: `operating_carrier + operating_flight_number + origin ICAO + original destination ICAO + initial_service_date + instance_initial_schedule_identity + collision discriminator` | Initial schedule identity distinguishes multiple same-number legs. Later schedule values never replace key material. The collision discriminator uses retained provider-record evidence only when necessary; it must not be a mutable schedule value. | Primary research identity; stable across all retime versions. No stable provider flight ID is assumed. |
| `callsign` | Where available | Disambiguation, not primary |
| `provider_flight_record_id` / `provider_record_key` | Deterministic identifier for the original provider record/FIDS appearance, retained even when operating identity is unresolved | Raw-record identity and collision disambiguation; not itself proof of a physical operated leg |
| Codeshare handling | When resolved, marketing numbers → `marketing_flight_numbers[]` on the confirmed operating leg. When `ambiguous_unknown`, retain `provider_flight_record_id`, provisional analytic identity, and `identity_resolution_status='ambiguous_codeshare'`; never silently coerce, merge, drop, or count it as a confirmed operating leg (see §7.2) | Prevents population inflation **and** false certainty |
| Retime handling | Every revision creates an append-only `schedule_version_id` with monotonic `retime_version`, old/new values, provider/availability clocks, and reason; `flight_instance_id` remains unchanged. A new flight instance requires positive distinct-leg evidence under a frozen resolver. Time displacement alone is insufficient. | Identity versus schedule state |
| Retime population assignment | Each FIDS population row retains the schedule version observable at its retrieval/cutoff under the stable flight instance. Never rewrite historical population membership to the final schedule. If distinct-leg identity is unresolved, mark/bound it rather than duplicating the denominator. | Denominator provenance |
| Cross-airport appearance | The same operated leg returned through selected origin and destination queries maps to one `flight_instance_id`, while each `(source_airport, service_interval, retrieval)` provenance row remains. Count the physical leg once where the analysis unit is flight and retain both query appearances for coverage diagnostics | Dedup without provenance loss |
| Diversion handling | Destination change → same `flight_instance_id` retains `original_scheduled_destination`; `current_operational_destination` and `actual_destination` updated; `diversion_flag` set | See §7.4 |
| Collision fallback | If fallback key collides (same carrier/number/origin/destination/date but distinct provider records) → append `provider_record_key` hash suffix | Uniqueness |
| Separation | `flight_instance_id` (stable) vs mutable `flight_state` (times, destinations, status) — never conflate | ETL principle |

Implementation requirement: the Log must identify the actual canonicalizer,
all production callers, schema constraints, migration, and identity tests. The
historical filenames are not accepted as evidence without repository proof.

### 7.2 Codeshare deduplication (binding)

- AeroDataBox `withCodeshared` can return inferred codeshares with **false results possible** (docs). Prefer enough raw information to canonicalize internally to operating leg.
- **Marketing identifiers stored as attributes**, never intentionally counted as separate physical legs. `codeshare_resolution_status = resolved_operator / resolved_marketing / ambiguous_unknown` is mandatory. `ambiguous_unknown` records are never silently coerced to an operating leg.
- Primary denominator reports both `confirmed_operating_leg_count` and `ambiguous_codeshare_record_count`; exact operating-leg population claims require the ambiguity to be resolved or explicitly bounded under a frozen rule.
- Verification at Gate 0.5: compare `withCodeshared=true` vs `false` payloads for the same airport-window; quantify ambiguity and prove canonicalization does not over-merge distinct physical flights.

### 7.3 Target-specific label observability (binding)

Generic `observed` flag retired. Each milestone has its own label-observed boolean:

| Column | Meaning | When true |
| ---- | ---- | ---- |
| `gate_out_label_observed` | verified `actual_gate_out` evidence exists | Evidence is verified as **actual** (not estimated), acquired by the target's recovery deadline, and its source/receipt/availability clocks are preserved |
| `wheels_off_label_observed` | verified `actual_wheels_off` evidence exists | Same target-specific acquisition rule; estimate-only runway time does not count |
| `wheels_on_label_observed` | verified `actual_wheels_on` evidence exists | Same target-specific acquisition rule; estimate-only runway time does not count |
| `gate_in_label_observed` | verified `actual_gate_in` evidence exists | Same target-specific acquisition rule; observing landing/wheels-on alone does not close gate-in |

A flight may be `wheels_on_label_observed=true` but `gate_in_label_observed=false` (gate time missing). Each regression target uses **its own observed population**: e.g. gate-arrival delay model trains only on `gate_in_label_observed=true` rows. Never train gate-in delay on wheels-on population.

### 7.4 Censoring with frozen grace (binding)

Binding sequence (patches all stale "window ended = missing" wording):

```text
collection/window ends
  → outcome NOT automatically declared missing
  → wait frozen grace interval (from §6.6, manifest)
  → check task-specific terminal evidence (see §7.3)
  → before deadline and no evidence: label_status=pending
  → target evidence present: label_status=observed
  → cancellation makes inapplicable movement targets label_status=not_applicable
  → survival endpoint with valid right-censoring: label_status=censored
  → deadline elapsed without usable evidence: label_status=missing
```

Grace measured at Gate 0.5 as `arrival-notification latency (received − wheels_on) P95 + margin`; frozen before Phase 6.

**Exact terminal-outcome acquisition protocol (binding):** A flight requires recovery when any selected target remains `pending` after its webhook window. Use the verified airport-FIDS endpoint under the same stored canonical query identity: persist `source_airport_icao`, `service_window_start/end`, local `fromLocal/toLocal`, IANA timezone, original `query_direction`, `population_role`, and flight-instance matching key from the population query. Recovery uses that bounded identity; it never issues an unbounded loose search. The protocol separately freezes `recovery_direction` (possibly `Both` only after Gate 0.5 proves role safety) and preserves the original primary/context role. Any diversion/destination query shape must be frozen before Phase 6. The reference arrival is the latest revised arrival that was available when recovery was scheduled, otherwise the original verified scheduled-arrival construct. Logical opportunities occur at reference arrival +30, +120, and +360 minutes; there are at most three per flight and at least 90 minutes between the first two. Compatible identities may be coalesced into pre-frozen due-time buckets, so one physical FIDS call may satisfy multiple flights. Each physical request follows the §5.1 maximum-three-total-attempt transport policy, and every attempt is reserved/debited **before issuing the HTTP request**. No fixed 20-unit shortcut is binding; `OUTCOME_REST_UNIT_BUDGET` is generated from the calendar and protocol. If the category cap cannot fund a call, DEFER/REFUSE without borrowing. Reaching the third logical opportunity **stops further recovery calls but does not terminalize an absent label**. The target remains `pending` until the frozen recovery deadline, which is reference arrival +24 hours for this protocol. At that deadline, verified terminal evidence produces `observed`/`not_applicable`; otherwise the target becomes `missing`, or `censored` only for a separately valid survival endpoint. Gate 0.5 must show that its measured grace fits inside this deadline; otherwise the protocol is BLOCKED for amendment. `CanceledUncertain` never fabricates cancellation. A diverted flight may have observed wheels-on while gate-in remains pending/missing. Every attempt records endpoint, query identity, request/response hash, retrieval/availability time, units, evidence, and decision. Prediction horizons T−24h/T−6h/T−90m are never post-flight retrieval times.

### 7.5 Route / OD and aircraft/tail identity (binding)

**Route/OD identity:** `directed OD = (origin ICAO, original_scheduled_destination ICAO)` — ordered pair, directed. Keep `original_scheduled_destination`, `operational_destination`, and `actual_destination` separately; diversion is `actual ≠ original` plus `diversion_flag`. Route uses the operating carrier, not a marketing codeshare. A retime does not change the route when destination is unchanged. Never silently convert directed to undirected.

**Aircraft/tail identity:** Priority fallback is `aircraft_reg` > `aircraft_mode_s` > `icao24` > verified provider aircraft ID, but `tail_known=true` only for a non-null verified registration. Missing or conflicting identifiers break the chain and are reported; never join on a fallback when conflict exists. An aircraft swap breaks the chain at the swap. Count `tail_chain_links` only on a consistent verified registration across legs within `max_turnaround=6h`; cross-midnight rotations are allowed. The next origin must equal the prior operational destination (or scheduled destination only under the frozen no-diversion rule).

**AIRBORNE / POST population denominator (NEW, blocking — the POST-side
population audit, mirroring §5):** "flights with airborne events we happened to observe"
is NOT a defined population. The **`airborne_eligible` denominator is
population-defined independent of airborne observation capture** — it comes
from the SAME `flight_population` layer as PRE (§5: provider-observable FIDS
population), intersected with **verified provider-native movement evidence** that
the flight became airborne. Permitted evidence is a FIDS operational state whose semantics are verified to imply departure, or a provider-native departure `runwayTime` only after **both runway semantics and actual-vs-estimated status** are verified. Estimated runway time cannot establish that the flight actually became airborne. A webhook point cannot define this
denominator. If neither independent criterion is constructible,
`AIRBORNE_DENOMINATOR=BLOCKED`. A flight is
`airborne_eligible` whether or not a single airborne point was ever captured —
using the airborne webhook to define the denominator would make it circular
with `airborne_observed`. Every eligible flight then goes through the same
measured funnel as PRE does, and each stage is counted and reported:

```text
airborne_eligible        = flight_population (FIDS-observable, §5)
                           ∩ verified_airborne_movement_evidence
                           [denominator defined from FIDS/population evidence,
                            NOT from whether we captured airborne points]
airborne_observed            ≥1 airborne observation captured for that flight
airborne_usable              ≥ N usable points (N frozen in manifest;
                             proposal ≥ 2 to fit a line, ≥ 5 to fit a curve)
airborne_trajectory_complete passes trajectory-completeness threshold
                             (completeness_% / max_gap_seconds, §6.2 QC)
POST_snapshot_eligible       produced ≥1 POST training snapshot (cutoff rule §6.1)
POST_labeled                 received a final terminal label (§6.2 terminalization)
```

- **Report the funnel at every monthly checkpoint:** `eligible → observed →
  usable → complete → snapshot_eligible → labeled`, e.g. as
  `airborne_eligible → airborne_observed → … → POST_labeled` proportions and
  per-airport / per-tier rows, exactly like the PRE population funnel (§5).
- **Missingness is measured, not hidden:** `airborne_eligible − airborne_observed`
  quantifies provider coverage; `observed − usable` quantifies point quality;
  `complete − snapshot_eligible` quantifies cutoff/cadence losses. If a big
  step collapses (e.g. most flights observed but few complete), that's a
  provider-cadence finding (Gate 0.5 / monthly), and POST training is weighted
  to the funnel denominators — a POST model must NOT be silently trained only
  on the easy-to-track tail of flights (`airborne_observed` disproportionately
  on provider-friendly aircraft/routes).
- POST-only rows from airports/feeds without independently constructible FIDS population membership are `POST_auxiliary=true`. They may support descriptive/auxiliary modeling but never denominator-based population claims.

## 8. Sampling design (LOCKED)

| Element | Decision |
| ---- | ---- |
| Default window | **1 × 4 h continuous** per day (preserves aircraft-chain continuity) |
| UTC schedule | Run-level **constrained randomized allocation**: seeded balanced permutation of `{00,04,08,12,16,20}`; every 6-day block uses each UTC slot exactly once (**HARD**); minimize weekday×UTC imbalance Σ(n_c−n̄)² among valid permutations (**SOFT**). Seeded + replayable (`time_window_schedule_seed`). Never claim "balanced at the weekday×UTC level" |
| Calendar | **26 × 1×4h + 3 × 2×2h + 2 × up-to-6h = 31 ordered batch-days.** `run_day_index=1..31` follows actual window start time. Washout may make these span more than 31 calendar dates; “Month 1” is only the first-cycle label. Approximate 84%/10%/6% figures are shares of batch-days, not elapsed calendar time, exposure-hours, or spend. |
| Crossover (R6) | Pair template frozen BEFORE treatment: freeze candidate pool → airport set → time/weekday class + eligible dates/split → exact pair contrast (control + one alternative) → randomize **order within the pair** → execute. The two shapes are part of the frozen contrast; code must not independently choose a shape per day. Treatment/order must NOT depend on any post-freeze observation |
| Environmental context (restored) | every batch/window records **raw/frozen weather components** (METAR/TAF-derived, §10), **ATC delay-program flags**, and **storm-track context** in batch metadata. A composite weather-severity index is used only if its deterministic formula is frozen; otherwise it remains NULL/DEFERRED. The crossover does not make the operating environment identical — matched windows can differ because severe weather arrived during one; that context must be explainable, not absorbed as "treatment effect". |
| Anchor pool | 5 **dual-eligible HUB-tier** airports; provisional KLAX/EGLL/WSSS/SBGR/OMDB only as candidate names, **finalized only after probing** (§9); one new draw/day, no-repeat-until-all for new draws, with an explicit paired-period-2 replay exception, order randomized via `anchor_pool_seed` |
| Anchor score | **40% exogenous traffic + 20% geo/network diversity + 20% carrier/international diversity + 20% standardized observed yield**; station/API capacity = separate feasibility GATE, not a score component; formula frozen in code pre-probe |
| Yield metric | `yield_score = f(unique_flights/credit, tail_chain_links/credit, stability)`, each standardized to [0,1], formula frozen pre-probe |
| REGIONAL | normalized yield-aware draw (`m_i ∈ [0.25,1.5]`, hard cap ×1.5, Σp=1); `airport_layer_design_probability = p_i` = conditional design probability at the draw; **adaptive rule is efficiency-oriented allocation, not representation-preserving**. Phase 6 starts from uniform `m_i=1.0`, EMA=NULL, and does **not** ingest probe yields into adaptive state; adaptation begins only from completed valid Phase-6 observations (§8.6) |
| Long-tail | named **"coverage floor"** (efficiency allocation, not representation) |
| `sampling_weight` | **NULL by default; no auto 1/p** (1/p ≠ valid flight-level inclusion probability) |

**ATC/storm environmental context:** Each batch/window records source-grounded context when available:
1. **Weather severity components:** preserve individual METAR/TAF variables (visibility, ceiling, wind/gust, precipitation, convective indicators). A composite `weather_severity_index` may be used only after a deterministic formula, source set, missing-data rule, and version are frozen; until then it is NULL/DEFERRED rather than an invented 0–5 score.
2. **ATC delay-program flags:** `ground_stop`, `ground_delay_program`, and other explicitly source-supported traffic-management flags, with `atc_source`, source timestamp, retrieval/available time, and missingness. FAA ATCSCC terminology must be used; unavailable programs remain NULL.
3. **Storm-track context:** source-grounded storm/radar variables only when a named NOAA product and deterministic spatial/temporal join are frozen; otherwise NULL.
4. **Derived summary:** `any_atc_program_active = true/false/NULL` may be derived from the verified ATC flags. The non-standard `MCD (Major Command Decision)` label is retired and must not appear in production schema or claims.

These context variables are used for:
- Crossover explanation (why two matched windows differ)
- Environmental covariates in model features
- Disruption-event classification only after the §6.2 event-source/taxonomy freeze
- Batch metadata audit trail

Record `environmental_context_json` on each batch row for full auditability.

### 8.1 Scheduler contract (code must REFUSE)

- Experiment day without a declared template/crossover group → REFUSED.
- Template/experiment mismatch (different tier mix or slots) → REFUSED.
- Crossover period-2 without its period-1 → REFUSED.
- `requested_window_hours=6` hitting the cap → tagged `up-to-6h`,
  `stop_reason='budget_reached'`, never relabeled "6h".
- Hard constraints (budget-day Alert ceiling ≤1900, one parent batch per `run_day_index`, prior budget day settled, valid window/tier/region,
  crossover integrity, no duplicate **new** anchor draw in a cycle; a reserved
  crossover period-2 replay is the only explicit repeat and is flagged) are never sacrificed for
  the soft balance objective.

### 8.2 REGIONAL adaptation full specification (binding)

Retain `m_i ∈ [0.25, 1.5]` and hard cap ×1.5, but freeze the recurrence:

| Item | Frozen value |
| ---- | ---- |
| Yield measure for adaptation | `yield_score_i` from the last completed **Phase-6** valid observation of `i` (same frozen §9 yield formula); if never observed in Phase 6 → EMA remains `NULL`, base `m_i=1.0`. Probe yields validate/select anchors but do not seed Phase-6 adaptation |
| EMA | For first valid nonempty observation, `ema_yield_i := yield_i`; thereafter `ema_yield_i := 0.5·yield_i + 0.5·previous_ema`. No approximate window changes this recurrence |
| Smoothing / cap | `raw_m = ema_yield_i / median_ema_yield_frame`; then `m_i = clamp(raw_m, 0.25, 1.5)` and `score_i = traffic_prior·m_i` capped `score_i ≤ traffic_prior·1.5` (no runaway) — **`r_i` REMOVED** |
| Update frequency | After each **completed** airport observation (not daily tick); `m_i` version incremented |
| Reference median | Median over currently `in_frame && pre_eligible && post_eligible` REGIONAL airports with non-NULL EMA and without `provider_failure`, `coverage_failed`, or `zero_yield_persistent`; prior valid EMA remains eligible for `zero_yield_repeated`. If pool empty or median ≤0, base `m_i=1.0` for all eligible airports |
| Cold start/missing | No valid EMA → base `m_i=1.0`; a missing observation does not update EMA or zero-yield state |
| Valid zero | Only a successful, complete provider observation with zero distinct canonical flight instances is a true zero. It advances the zero-yield FSM but does not update EMA. Provider/subscription/coverage failure sets its separate failure field and does not advance the FSM |
| Repeated-zero penalty | While the current state is `zero_yield_repeated`, use `m_i=clamp(base_m_i·0.75,0.25,1.5)`; `normal`, `once`, and `persistent` apply no numeric zero penalty, with persistent excluded from the median pending review. A successful observation returns the state to `normal` and removes this transient penalty through a versioned recomputation. |
| Zero-yield response | See §8.3 — only `repeated/persistent` state may down-weight; `once` never down-weights |
| Reset | EMA history is never reset by a clock or batch boundary; the transient repeated-zero penalty is recomputed from the current state and therefore disappears on a successful observation. Manual adjudication is required for any persistent/coverage state change; every transition is appended to `m_i_history`. |
| Deterministic replay | Sorted frozen state/history determines scores and probabilities; the frozen `random_seed` determines only the weighted selection. Persist a canonical state hash, probability vector, seed, and selected ICAO on the frame/batch records identified by the actual schema. |

All parameters (`α`, caps, window) in `adb_collection_meta` (`adaptive_version`, `adaptive_hash`).

### 8.3 Zero-yield state machine (binding)

The following state machine is the sole current zero-yield rule:

| State | Entry condition | Leaves frame? | Affects `m_i`? | Next state |
| ---- | ---- | ---- | ---- | ---- |
| `normal` | Last observation had ≥1 flight row | No | No (normal `m_i` path) | `zero_yield_once` on first empty |
| `zero_yield_once` | 1 consecutive empty observation | No | **NO** — excluded from adaptive evidence (single empty could be outage) | `zero_yield_repeated` on 2nd consecutive empty; `normal` on success |
| `zero_yield_repeated` | ≥2 consecutive empties | No | **YES, candidate for down-weight** (`m_i ×0.75` soft penalty) | `zero_yield_persistent` after ≥5 consecutive empties or 30 days of empties |
| `zero_yield_persistent` | Persistent empties (see above) | No | **Excluded from adaptive evidence** (like `once`) — log for manual review, still selectable via coverage floor | Manual review may move to `coverage_failed` if feed error proven |
| `coverage_failed` | Feed/probe error (not empty) | **YES** — may leave frame | N/A | — |
| `stale` | No direct observation >60 days | No | No | — |

One empty observation never removes airport; `coverage_failed` is the only exit.

### 8.4 Anchor / HUB relationship (binding)

When `ADB_ANCHOR_ENABLED=true`, the single rotating anchor **consumes the one HUB slot** in `{HUB:1, MID:2, REGIONAL:1}`. That is: each batch has 1 anchor-HUB (from the 5-pool, `no-repeat-until-all` for new draws, `anchor_pool_seed`; a reserved crossover period-2 replay is not a new draw), 2 MID (freshest-first per §8.6), 1 REGIONAL (yield-aware draw §8.2). Code and docs must match; `pickAirportCandidates` enforces exactly 1 HUB when anchor enabled, otherwise draws HUB via freshest-first.

### 8.5 HUB and MID selection mechanics (binding)

| Tier | Selection | Freshness rule | Eligibility filter | Tie-break |
| ---- | ---- | ---- | ---- | ---- |
| HUB (anchor) | `anchor_pool` rotation per §8.4 | `no-repeat-until-all` 5-cycle for new draws, seeded `anchor_pool_seed`; reserved `PAIR_REPLAY` is the only repeat | `pre_eligible && post_eligible && in_frame` | Lexical ICAO if tie |
| HUB (if anchor disabled) | Freshest-first | `ORDER BY last_direct_observation_at ASC NULLS FIRST, tier_prior DESC` | Same | Same |
| MID | Freshest-first (deterministic) | `ORDER BY last_direct_observation_at ASC NULLS FIRST` with `recent_exclusion=7 days` (no MID re-picked within 7 days unless frame < 2× slots) | Same | Same |

Eligibility: `in_frame=true` + required feeds (§4.4) + not in active batch + cooldown. No unspecified randomness for MID.

**Tier×region allocation (binding):** A batch has logical slot IDs
`HUB`, `MID_A`, `MID_B`, and `REGIONAL`. Region is chosen before the airport;
the selector may not draw globally and merely report region afterward.

1. At FREEZE, derive `eligible_region_set_<slot>` from nonempty dual-eligible
   tier×**mapped**-region cells (`region != UNMAPPED`) and record excluded/empty
   cells. The HUB/anchor sequence
   is the region sequence induced by the frozen five-airport anchor rotation.
2. For `MID_A`, `MID_B`, and `REGIONAL`, the calendar solver materializes a
   seeded balanced permutation over that slot's eligible region set. In every
   complete block of `|eligible_region_set|` **new template draws**, each region
   appears exactly once; the remainder is determined by a frozen derived seed.
   The two MID slots use distinct derived seeds and cannot select the same
   airport in a batch.
3. Crossover period 2 replays period 1's frozen airport set and consumes no new
   region draw. The region ledger records template draw versus replay.
4. After a target region is fixed, airport selection follows the anchor,
   freshest-first, or REGIONAL `m_i` rule **within that tier×region cell**.
   If no candidate remains at execution time, return `REFUSED_CELL_EMPTY`; do
   not substitute another region/tier or a POST-only airport.
5. Freeze and hash the complete slot→region sequence with the calendar. Report
   planned and realized slot×region counts, refusals, and replays. Never claim
   flight-level representativeness from this balanced allocation.

### 8.6 Coverage floor (no-starvation) (binding)

Mathematically, every eligible REGIONAL airport requires `traffic_prior_i>0` and `m_i≥0.25`; otherwise frame validation fails. Define `adaptive_score_i=traffic_prior_i·m_i` after the §8.2 zero-yield rule. If the airport has no qualifying Phase-6 direct observation or its last qualifying observation is ≥20 days old, set `coverage_floor_eligible=true` and `coverage_boost_i=1.5`; otherwise set `coverage_boost_i=1.0`. At every draw compute `draw_score_i=adaptive_score_i·coverage_boost_i` and `p_i=draw_score_i/Σdraw_score`. An unsuccessful draw does not clear eligibility. Only a successful valid Phase-6 direct observation resets coverage age/boost. The boost never overwrites `m_i` and remains an efficiency/coverage preference, not a representation claim.

**Coverage clarification:** positive probability—even with a boost—does not guarantee finite-run selection. If many/all airports are overdue, a common multiplier may not materially change normalized probabilities. Report floor eligibility, effective boosted score/probability, coverage age, and realized selection frequency separately; never call this deterministic starvation prevention.

**Adaptive determinism:** `m_i`, zero-yield state, eligibility, boost, scores,
and probabilities must be deterministic from the same frozen configuration and
history. EMA uses fixed `α=0.5`; clamp/penalty/boost rules contain no random
branch. Only the final weighted draw uses the frozen random seed. Replay the
same state/history/seed twice and require identical states, probabilities, and
selection; record the artifact hash in the manifest.

**Phase-6 adaptive initial state:** Before Phase 6 begins, the initial state of the adaptive mechanism MUST be frozen:
1. `m_i_initial_source='uniform'`; every eligible REGIONAL airport starts `m_i=1.0`.
2. `ema_initial_source='none'`; every EMA starts NULL. Probe results do not seed Phase-6 adaptive state.
3. `zero_yield_initial_state='normal'` for every airport; probe results do not seed it.
4. Every never-observed eligible airport starts `coverage_floor_eligible=true` and `coverage_boost_i=1.5`; the common multiplier may leave initial normalized probabilities unchanged. Coverage age is NULL/never-observed until the first successful valid Phase-6 observation.
5. The initial state and update function are FROZEN before Phase 6. During the run, `ema_yield_i` evolves from completed valid observations and `m_i` is deterministically recomputed from that EMA using the frozen function. Do NOT re-initialize `m_i`; provider errors are not zero-yield observations.

### 8.7 Crossover specification (binding)

| Item | Frozen value |
| ---- | ---- |
| `crossover_group_id` | **One unique matched pair ID** (`pair_001`…`pair_005`); do not reuse a 14-day block ID as the pair identity. `crossover_block_id` is a separate optional broader scheduling/blocking field. |
| Exact pair composition | **Exactly five pairs** in the 31-batch-day calendar: **3 pairs** compare `1×4h control` vs `2×2h alternative`; **2 pairs** compare `1×4h control` vs `up-to-6h alternative`. Thus five of the 26 four-hour batch-days are crossover controls and the other 21 are ordinary 4h batch-days. |
| Experimental/randomization unit | **Batch-day within a frozen pair** — not flight and not four independent airport-days. The pair is the blocking/matched-analysis unit. |
| Matching variables | **Same frozen airport set for both paired treatment periods**, same `time_class`, same `weekday_class`, and the same evaluation split partition, all subject to ≥24h end→start washout. These are HARD pair constraints; UNSAT rather than relaxation. Period 2 replays the reserved airport set. |
| Treatment | Pair template freezes exactly one contrast: `1×4h vs 2×2h` or `1×4h vs up-to-6h`. |
| Randomization | Seeded `crossover_seed` randomizes **order within the pair** (control-first vs alternative-first) after the pair template/contrast is frozen; it does not independently choose arbitrary shapes for each day. |
| Template freeze | Freeze candidate pool + airport set + time/weekday class + pair contrast + eligible dates/partition before treatment order randomization; treatment never depends on post-freeze observation. |
| Incomplete pair handling | If either period fails materially, the pair is `unmatched/incomplete` and excluded from the primary within-pair crossover contrast; both periods remain in operational diagnostics as applicable. |
| Order/carryover | Order balanced as closely as solver constraints permit; ≥24h end-to-start washout between repeated-airport periods. |
| Tagging | `crossover_group_id`, optional `crossover_block_id`, `crossover_period (1/2)`, `pair_role=control/alternative`, `window_shape`, `actual_window_hours`, `stop_reason` on `adb_collection_batches` |

**Crossover randomization/analysis unit (corrected):** Each period is one parent batch-day, so all airports in that period share the assigned pair role/window shape. Airport/flight observations are clustered within batch-day and are not independent randomized units. For `2×2h`, both child segments retain one parent assignment. Record `randomization_unit='batch-day-within-pair'`, `analysis_cluster_unit='batch-day'`, `paired_analysis_block='crossover_group_id'`, and parent/child IDs. The primary crossover estimate is the **within-pair contrast** across the five matched pairs; uncertainty/SE logic must respect pair blocking and batch-day clustering.

**Washout definition:** Require at least 24h from the END of the previous treatment window to the START of the next treatment for the same airport. A Monday 08:00-12:00 window therefore permits the next start no earlier than Tuesday 12:00; Tuesday 08:00 is only a 20h end-to-start gap. Record exact `washout_start`, `washout_end`, and computed duration on each batch.

For `2×2h`, store one `experiment_day_id` and `parent_batch_id` with two child `segment_id` rows containing `segment_start`, `segment_end`, `gap_start`, `gap_end`, and `window_shape`. Both segments use the same frozen airport set and parent treatment. Create separate subscriptions per segment and delete/settle them before the gap; no subscription remains active in the gap. Build FIDS population independently for each half-open segment. Flights only in the gap are `captured_outside_population=true` and cannot enter the treatment denominator. Attribute item costs to the receiving segment and shared setup costs to the parent under a frozen allocation rule; terminal outcomes continue by flight-instance identity regardless of segment end.

**Crossover feasibility (P0 accounting separation):** Alert credits and REST/API units are separate. Each parent batch-day/`budget_day_id` has `HARD_CAP=1900`; both child segments of a 2×2h treatment share that one cap. The paired period has another budget-day cap, and both draw from the run ceiling. FIDS uses the monthly REST/API-unit ledger, never the 1,900 Alert cap. The solver proves per-budget-day Alert feasibility and REST-total feasibility or returns `UNSAT`.

**Pair replay vs ordinary cooldown:** period 2 of a matched crossover is a reserved replay of the period-1 frozen airport set. Normal MID 7-day freshness and anchor-cycle selection are applied when creating the pair template, not re-applied to redraw period 2. This is the sole permitted anchor repeat: mark `anchor_draw_type='PAIR_REPLAY'` and `anchor_replay=true`; no arbitrary repeat is allowed. The solver still enforces ≥24h end-to-start washout for every repeated airport.

**Calendar solver contract:** Before Phase 6, generate and freeze all 31 batch-days rather than using hand-written dates. The solver must enforce window-shape totals, **exact five-pair composition (3 control-vs-2×2h, 2 control-vs-up-to-6h)**, six UTC slots and each 6-day block balance, the §8.5 slot×region sequences, weekday/weekend and time-class matching, ≥24h end-to-start washout, pair order, **both periods of each pair in the same train/validation/test partition**, anchor/tier-slot rules, batch-day assignment, billing/run dates, and budget feasibility. It returns a complete schedule plus constraint evidence or `UNSAT` with failed constraints; no hard constraint is silently relaxed. Treatment/randomization, analysis, and cluster unit are all `batch-day`; child airport/flight rows are clustered observations, not independent assignments. Assignment may use only the frozen template, never post-freeze yield, weather, disruption, or outcomes. The production solver, SAT/UNSAT tests, and actual calendar artifact are mandatory offline work; the paired Log currently records `NOT_VERIFIED_REPOSITORY_UNAVAILABLE`.

### 8.8 Scheduler tie-break (binding)

Run-level constrained randomized allocation uses one pre-frozen `time_window_schedule_seed`. When candidate schedules under that seed have equal `Σ(n_c−n̄)²` weekday×UTC imbalance, choose the lexicographically smallest canonical serialized schedule (ordered date, slot, shape, pair ID). The seed is an input, not a tie-break candidate. No optional region/local-time objective may appear unless frozen as a binding soft objective. If no schedule satisfies every hard constraint, return `UNSAT` and create no window.

---

## 9. Two-stage anchor probe (budget-capped)

1. **Stage 1:** **Exact 12 dual-eligible HUB-tier** shortlisted candidates across regions (list frozen in the hash-locked `preprobe_reference_freeze_record` before paid execution and embedded unchanged into the final manifest), target-duration 2h cap-censored probes at matched `time_class`/`weekday_class`, sequentially. Record unique-flight-instances/credit, chain-links/credit, and stability. WSSS and OMAA are included in the 12 and use the same cap-censored protocol. If fewer than 12 such candidates exist after the frozen frame is built, Gate 2 is `BLOCKED/UNSAT`; do not fill the shortlist with MID, REGIONAL, POST-only, or unclassified airports.
2. **Stage 2:** The top 5 capacity-passing candidates by anchor_score get a cap-censored confirmation probe. If fewer than 5 pass Stage 1, Gate 2 remains incomplete: add candidates from the predeclared replacement list, run Stage 1 on each, rerank, then Stage-2-confirm every candidate entering the final five.
3. Final anchor pool is exactly 5 only after all five pass required confirmation; no unconfirmed replacement may enter. Station/API capacity is a feasibility gate `rows_per_hour ≥60` before scoring. The authorized probe-day Alert ceiling is 500 credits; because billing occurs on SEND, admission and stop decisions must use the pre-probe-smoke-derived `unsettled_burst_margin_credits`, and any settled overshoot is a protocol deviation that blocks further probes.

**Stage-1/Stage-2 rules:**
- **Stage-1 duration:** Target 2.0 hours, ending earlier at the SEND-aware probe soft stop or any safety/failure stop. Watchdog polling cadence is frozen before paid use; the old fixed 60-second tick is not accepted unless offline tests and pre-probe evidence show it is safe. Record actual duration, stop reason, and `duration_censored=true` when truncated.
- **Stage-2 promotion/replacement:** Promote the top 5 capacity-pass candidates. If fewer than 5 pass, Gate 2 is incomplete; consume the frozen replacement list sequentially, Stage-1 probe and rerank replacements, then Stage-2-confirm every final-five candidate. Do not fabricate or admit an unconfirmed candidate.
- **Stage-2 duration:** Target 4.0 hours, ending earlier under the same SEND-aware stop rules; record censoring identically.
- **Budget:** `PROBE_CAP_DAILY=500` is the cumulative ceiling for one `probe_budget_day_id`, not per candidate. A probe may not cross UTC midnight unless the authorization and ledger explicitly split it into two budget IDs; default is refuse such a window. Settle one probe budget day before starting another. Reserve against both probe-day and pre-run ledgers; a settled total above 500 is `MISMATCH/PROTOCOL_DEVIATION` and pauses paid work.
- **Scheduling:** Sequential, never crossing real time. Each probe must complete before the next starts. Record `probe_start_utc`, `probe_end_utc`, `probe_duration_minutes` for each probe.

### 9.1 Anchor probe exactness (binding)

| Parameter | Frozen value |
| ---- | ---- |
| Shortlist count | **12** candidates — list in the pre-probe freeze record (`anchor_shortlist_v1`) with exogenous values, source, retrieval date, hash (see §4.1/4.2/4.5 for traffic/region/carrier sources) |
| Shortlist membership | Must include WSSS and OMAA; every shortlist and replacement entry must be `tier=HUB`, `in_frame=true`, `pre_eligible=true`, and `post_eligible=true`. Remaining ten and ordered replacement list are BLOCKED until final frame/reference variables are frozen; if twelve qualifying HUB candidates cannot be enumerated, Gate 2 is `BLOCKED/UNSAT`; no paid probe before both lists and hashes exist |
| Stage 1 duration | Target 2.0 hours; stop at the SEND-aware 500-credit probe-budget-day soft stop and mark `duration_censored=true` when truncated |
| Stage 2 promotion count | Exactly 5 after capacity-pass replacement and confirmation protocol completes |
| Stage 2 duration | Target 4.0 hours; same cap-censoring rule |
| Capacity gate | `rows_per_hour ≥ 60`; the implementation must expose this canonical constant and the manifest must hash it. A path/name is not evidence until recorded in the Log. |
| Time-class | `UTC slot ±1 hour` (e.g. slot 08:00 allows probes 07:00-09:00) |
| Weekday-class | `weekday (Mon-Fri) vs weekend (Sat-Sun)` matched |
| Scheduling | `matched time-class/weekday-class`, sequential, never crossing real time; production `anchor_probe.ts:runSingleProbe` **must enforce this and be verified before paid probing** |
| Yield count unit | Confirmed distinct physical `flight_instance_id` observed during actual exposure; retries/updates never create flights. Preserve `confirmed_unique_lower` and `confirmed_plus_ambiguous_upper`. Use the conservative lower bound for scoring, and report both. Final-five membership must be invariant under the recorded ambiguity bounds; otherwise Gate 2 is `INSUFFICIENT_IDENTITY_RESOLUTION`, not a guessed ranking. Chain links require confirmed compatible flight/tail identity. |
| Ranking/tie | Capacity-pass first, then descending `anchor_score`; exact score tie breaks by lexical ICAO |
| Stage-2 failure | Failed/invalid candidate is removed. Next ranked capacity-pass candidate may replace it only after that candidate completes valid Stage-2 confirmation; otherwise Gate 2 remains incomplete |
| Replacement protocol | Frozen ordered replacement list; each entrant runs Stage 1, all valid candidates rerank, and every final-five member requires valid Stage 2 |

### 9.2 Yield-reference normalization (binding)

The old word **"calibration"** for anchor yield is renamed **"yield-reference normalization"** to distinguish from probabilistic model calibration (ECE/Brier in §13.1).

- **Primary reference:** WSSS (Singapore Changi) — `reference_yield_wsss` measured with the identical target-2h, 500-cap-censored protocol.
- **Fallback reference:** OMAA (Abu Dhabi) — used only if WSSS `capacity_gate` fails or WSSS probe invalid; `reference_yield_omaa`.
- **Diagnostic:** Both probed even when one is primary; report `wsss_vs_omaa_ratio`.
- **Invalid-reference behavior:** If both references fail capacity gate → probe aborts, manifest records `reference_invalid`, no scoring.

**Yield standardization formula (binding):**

```text
component_std = clamp(candidate_component / reference_component, 0, 1)
where candidate_component ∈ {uf_per_credit, chain_per_credit, stability}
      reference_component = reference_yield_wsss.component (or omaa if fallback)
      clamp(x,0,1) = min(1, max(0, x))
```

Each required `reference_component` must be finite and strictly positive. Use
the OMAA component only when the corresponding WSSS component is invalid and
the pre-frozen fallback rule permits it. If both are missing, non-finite, or
zero, that component—and therefore anchor scoring—is `NOT_SCORABLE`; Gate 2
remains incomplete. Never divide by zero, insert an after-the-fact epsilon, or
drop/reweight a component after seeing candidate results.

Clipping justification: values above reference saturate at 1.0 — intentional, because anchor yield is a **minority 20% component** of `anchor_score`; saturation prevents a single lucky probe from dominating (see §8 anchor score).

**Anchor score (binding, frozen):**
```text
anchor_score = 0.40·traffic_score + 0.20·geo_score + 0.20·carrier_score + 0.20·yield_score
yield_score  = (uf_std + chain_std + stability_std) / 3
traffic_score = per §4.5 exogenous traffic normalized
geo_score, carrier_score per §4.5
```
Station/API capacity is a **feasibility gate** before scoring, never a score component.

**Stability calculation (binding, exposure-correct under cap censoring):**
```text
bucket_width = 15 minutes
use only complete 15-minute buckets fully contained in the actual active probe interval
count_i = distinct canonical flight instances whose first valid observation in this probe falls in bucket i
n_buckets = number of complete observed buckets
mean = Σcount_i / n_buckets
variance = Σ(count_i - mean)² / n_buckets   # population variance across observed buckets
SD = sqrt(variance)
CV = SD / mean   (if mean=0 → stability=0)
stability = 1 / (1 + CV)
```
Retries/updates never recount a flight. An unobserved portion after a cap stop is **not** filled with zero buckets. Freeze `min_stability_buckets` before paid probes; fewer complete buckets yields `INSUFFICIENT_SAMPLE` for stability rather than a fabricated score. The final partial bucket is excluded unless an explicit exposure-normalized rule is frozen before Stage 1.

---

## 10. Weather (LOCKED)

- METAR/TAF → `observation_time` / `issue_time` ≤ cutoff; a TAF issued at T−2
  is never used for a T−24h prediction.
- **Forecast-as-known-at-cutoff (NEW, blocking):** every forecasted weather
  input preserves `source`, `issue_time`, `retrieval_time`,
  `information_available_timestamp` (when OUR layer had it, §6.1),
  `valid_from`, `valid_to`, and `amendment/revision id` where available. The
  model sees the forecast **as it existed at the cutoff** — never the
  meteorological truth that became known later (TAF amendments must not leak a
  revised forecast into a snapshot taken before the amendment was issued).
  Retrospective archive availability ≠ eligibility (§6.1).
- Sources (free, no AeroDataBox credit cost — retrieval/storage/archive are
  separate engineering constraints): aviationweather.gov METAR/TAF
  (operational; current Data API states **access to up to the previous 30 days** — this is retrieval-history depth, not a retention/license permission; verify at freeze; cache files), NOAA GFS/NAM grids, and ERA5 for retrospective truth or explicitly lagged historical features. Unidata LDM means Local Data Manager, a delivery mechanism; name the actual NOAA product when LDM/IDD is used.
- Schema: `weather_observation` + `weather_forecast` tables; `source` tag
  (`live_metar` | `archive_metar` | `gfs` | `nam` | `era5`) so depths never mix.
- **Weather-availability gate (new):** weather feature availability is itself
  measured and reported; missing historical weather is never silently filled
  from a later/revised source. The layer is "architected" day 1, "complete"
  only after the archive coverage check passes.
- Enters the ladder at **Model 2**; marginal value = Model2 − Model1 on Engine A.

### 10.1 Weather hierarchy and joins (binding)

| Item | Frozen value |
| ---- | ---- |
| Precedence | `live_metar` (aviationweather.gov operational) > `archive_metar` > the frozen operational grid source (`gfs` or `nam`) — first available at `available_at ≤ cutoff` wins; a deeper source never overwrites a shallower available source. **ERA5 is not in the operational fallback chain**; it is retrospective truth or a separately verified lagged feature under §10.1/§12.2, never a substitute for missing current weather. |
| Spatial join | Nearest operational station within **30 km** of airport reference point; if none, operational fallback may use the appropriate GFS/NAM grid cell. ERA5 is never the current operational fallback. |
| Temporal join | Snapshot at `T` uses most recent observation with `available_at ≤ T` and `valid_from ≤ T ≤ valid_to` (for forecasts) |
| Issue/amendment selection | For TAF, latest amendment with `issue_time ≤ cutoff` and `available_at ≤ cutoff`; never a later amendment's truth |
| Missing behavior | No observation within 6h → `weather_missing=true`, `weather_source=NULL`; not imputed |
| Archive/live distinction | `source` tag preserves origin; era vs retrieval time stored separately |
| Versioning | `weather_source_version`, `weather_api_date_checked`, `weather_retrieval_date`, `weather_archive_depth_verified_at_freeze` in manifest |

**Weather observation-time correction:** When using METAR/TAF data, `observation_time` is the physical time the weather was observed, not necessarily the time the report became available. Use `observation_time`/forecast valid time for **physical temporal alignment**, while the independent leakage gate remains `available_at ≤ prediction_cutoff_utc` (and `issue_time ≤ cutoff` for forecasts). A METAR physically observed at 13:55 but not available to our system until 14:02 cannot enter a 14:00 snapshot. `report_time`/retrieval clocks are preserved separately rather than substituted for observation time. The Unidata Local Data Manager (LDM) is data-distribution software; when referencing LDM/IDD delivery, name the actual meteorological product delivered (e.g., NEXRAD Level II, GFS GRIB), not LDM itself.

**ERA5 availability rule (P0):** ERA5 is retrospective reanalysis, not an operational forecast fallback. A fixed heuristic such as `era5_timestamp ≤ cutoff−5d` is **not proof** that a particular release was published and available to our system by the cutoff. ERA5 may enter an as-known-at-cutoff model feature only if the exact product/version has a verifiable release/publication time and our `available_at ≤ prediction_cutoff_utc`; otherwise it is restricted to retrospective diagnostics/truth and cannot replace missing operational weather. GFS/NAM similarly preserve `model_run/issue_time`, `valid_time`, retrieval time, and `available_at` separately; `valid_time` is not issue/release time.

### 10.2 Weather retrieval depth + source retention/compliance (binding)

AviationWeather.gov currently states that its weather database permits API access to **up to the previous 30 days**. Treat this only as `weather_api_history_depth_verified`; it is **not** a statement that the project may or must retain weather for exactly 30 days. Freeze `weather_source_version`/`docs_API_date`/`retrieval_date`/`weather_api_history_depth_verified` in the manifest; source/license retention rights are a separate verification.

**Data-retention verification:** Verify data retention for every source before the first paid operation that stores its content, and re-verify the full design before Phase 6:

**⚠ PERMANENT_RAW_RETENTION = BLOCKED — DO NOT CLAIM PERMANENT RETENTION WITHOUT VERIFIED PLAN TERMS ⚠**

Current AeroDataBox Terms (last updated August 21, 2026; rechecked 2026-09-02 at `https://aerodatabox.com/terms`, Article 5.5) describe a retention ceiling based on the greater of seven calendar days, Cache-Control `max-age`, or a period explicitly granted in applicable Plan Terms, plus deletion after the permitted purpose. Article 5.6 treats genuine Derived Works differently; it does **not** let this project self-classify arbitrary normalized copies as Derived Works. This Plan is an engineering control, not legal advice. The actual subscription channel/Plan Terms and table/column classifications require owner review, so `plan_name`, `raw_content_retention_allowed_days`, `raw_content_expiry_policy`, `derived_work_classification`, and `permanent_retention_explicitly_allowed` remain BLOCKED_LIVE/HUMAN-LEGAL. While raw content is lawfully retained it is immutable; auditable compliance expiry/deletion is required. No paid probe may start until a pre-probe retention record covers every content class that probe will create; Phase 6 additionally requires the full retention design.

1. **AeroDataBox FIDS/Schedule raw Contents:** legal storage/retention is governed by AeroDataBox Terms + the actual subscribed Plan Terms, not by whatever historical range the API happens to expose. Provider retrieval-history depth/purge behavior, if operationally relevant, is a separate field from our retention right.
2. **AeroDataBox webhook notification raw Contents:** raw delivery/item content is append-only/immutable **while lawfully retained**; Article 5.5/Plan-Term expiry applies unless a longer permitted period is explicitly established. Compliance deletion must not be implemented as semantic UPDATE of retained raw evidence; use an auditable non-content tombstone/hash only where permitted.
3. **AeroDataBox airborne/raw provider Contents:** same raw-Contents rule. Normalized/feature/model rows are not automatically exempt: each important table/column must be classified as `raw_api_content`, `derived_work`, or `non_aerodatabox_metadata`, with the legal basis recorded. Article 5.6's exception for **AeroDataBox Terms Article 5.5** applies to genuine Derived Works; the project may not self-declare arbitrary copies as Derived Works.
4. **Weather (METAR/TAF):** current AviationWeather API history depth is up to 30 days; **retention/storage rights are separate** and follow the applicable source/license/policy. Capture/archive strategy must preserve as-known-at-cutoff evidence without treating API history depth as a license term.
5. **Database rows:** retention is classification-specific. Do not apply one blanket `retention_period_days` to every database row. Raw AeroDataBox Contents follow the verified raw-content legal basis; genuine Derived Works and non-AeroDataBox metadata record their own basis/policy.
6. For every retained source/table class record at minimum `content_classification`, `retention_verified_date`, `retention_source`, `retention_legal_basis`, `retention_period_days_or_condition`, and `expiry_action`. Unknown required legal basis blocks FREEZE for the affected content class.

**Pre-paid security/retention gate (P0):** Before any paid FIDS call, safety
smoke, probe, canary, or pilot that persists provider content, record and test:

- applicable Terms/Plan/channel evidence and a content-class retention matrix;
- an expiry job plus backup/replica/object-store propagation, dry-run evidence,
  and an approved deletion/tombstone rule that leaves no raw content in logs;
- TLS webhook URL, any provider-supported authentication/verification mechanism
  (or an explicit no-auth limitation with an owner-approved compensating
  control), replay-safe delivery identity, secret-manager use, least-privilege
  database role, encryption at rest where supported, and access/audit logging;
- redaction proving API keys, webhook secrets, authorization headers, and raw
  payloads are absent from ordinary command logs/error telemetry;
- incident response: authentication, persistence, reconciliation, or deletion
  failure immediately disables new subscriptions and requires human review.

Unknown/failed evidence is `PREPAID_SECURITY_RETENTION=BLOCKED`; it is not
waived because a run is small. A human may authorize spend only after this
record and all earlier offline prerequisites pass.

Before FREEZE, a machine-readable readiness record must set `history_infrastructure_ready`, `historical_feature_sources_verified`, `weather_operational_sources_verified`, `weather_as_known_at_cutoff_test_pass`, and `retention_terms_verified`, each with evidence hash/time. Any false/unknown required field blocks the affected feature and FREEZE: values remain NULL with explicit missingness, and no future-known backfill is allowed. Current values are false/unknown; readiness is BLOCKED.
